# Arany beta implementation

## Metadata

- Status: `In progress`
- Owner: Codex
- Last updated: 2026-10-06

## Goal

Ship the CLI-only Arany beta defined in [the canonical architecture](../../docs/architecture/system-overview.md): durable Sessions and Runs, bounded direct children, deterministic replay and output, safe Workspace inputs, supported Providers, an accessible inline terminal, opt-in trace export, and explicitly enabled guarded coding Tools, commands, Skills and local MCP.

## Current milestone and order (2026-10-04)

### Remaining work and handoff

This is the current remaining-work list; the completed slices below retain their verification history. Follow the [beta 1 verification-handoff rule](../../agents/testing.md#evidence-lanes): complete implementation with available local/synthetic evidence and leave user-owned checks until the final handoff. The local Linux executable, offline Engine direct/team turns and replay, setup model/effort selection, and current dependency-license policy already pass their recorded checks. [Next steps](../../NEXT_STEPS.md) separates beta 1 checks, the Anthropic subscription route, and beta 2/release work.

### Implemented: remember saved-account model choices

The user requested automatic reuse of the last selected model and acquired catalog across new starts and confirms that model recovery now works. Persist token-free preferences in one bounded private account-root file keyed by exact Provider and saved-account UUID. Keep at most eight sources and 4,096 bounded model IDs per source within a 1-MiB aggregate file, evicting oldest sources when needed. Store only IDs and the selected model/effort; rebuild native capability labels from current compiled metadata. Custom profiles retain their declared model and fresh evidence; explicit environment-backed flags, resume/fork semantics, account/billing-route choice and Run credential/consent admission keep their existing owners.

On a new bare start, restore the matching saved-account tuple and seed its cached catalog without keyring, refresh, inference or hidden model check. A typed/picker model choice and successful setup remember the acknowledged tuple; successful catalog reads remember the rows. Cached rows remain staging and `/model` refreshes them through its existing cancellable owner. Invalid/unsafe preferences are not authority and produce recoverable guidance; a preference write failure reports that remembering failed without rolling back canonical Session defaults. No previous account's selection is inherited by a replacement UUID. Existing explicit choices and resumed Session defaults win.

Verification map: extend existing private-file Store safety corpus for this fixed leaf; add a bounded preference transition corpus for restart, account isolation, tuple validation, quotas/eviction and corrupted cache. Extend the existing isolated ChatGPT product journey with bare restart preserving selection and cached Tab/catalog behavior without requests. Run affected slash/model/setup owners and consolidated repository checks. The terminal spec owns these accepted observable scenarios; update setup documentation and architecture ownership. Security pass: remote model IDs are untrusted and bounded, the checked account-root handle and replacement lock own atomic durable writes, no secret or capability evidence is persisted, and account/consent/TLS/usage checks remain required before Workspace disclosure. Auto-learning belongs in that spec and the existing Store/CLI rules. The private-record corpus also verifies stale catalog refresh preserving the newest tuple, source-count and aggregate-byte eviction, and reopening through checked file handles. The existing native fixture now expects remembered defaults and explicitly empties its token-free catalog cache before its cold-loading/draft gate; busy catalog notices do not announce an idle input prompt. These fixture changes preserve that original loading claim alongside cached startup.

### Completed: bounded product-test observation

The existing custom product owner reproduces a valid JSONL export blocking beyond the unchanged ten-second parent deadline: the old observer waited for exit before draining its pipes. The fixture creates reduced Linux pipes before spawning the product, asserts its expected export exceeds pipe capacity, and compares exact output, empty stderr and unchanged closed/reopened canonical Events. A first fixture attempt exceeded the existing title limit and was corrected without changing product admission. With concurrent draining, the original owner passes exactly one completed test in 0.38 seconds; the attached automatic-compaction success/failure/interruption owner also passes exactly one test. This is observation reliability, not a product latency benchmark.

Custom and compaction callers now reuse the current Unix observer. Existing Rustix makes both pipes nonblocking; each capture is capped at 64 KiB and finite reads return to one ten-second deadline covering process status and EOF. Overflow fails visibly under direct-child kill/reap ownership. Reader threads are not added because inherited pipes could block their joins. Already-taken PTY channels and callers' descendant/namespace guards retain separate ownership; the observer does not acquire process-tree authority. Other shared fixture operations remain Linux-only. Assets are test-private canonical data and bounded output; fixed executables, cleared environments and synthetic fixtures remain the authority. No host credential/clipboard service, new module, dependency, production behavior, output limit or request permission is introduced. Testing guidance records the pipe/deadline invariant using Writing for Agents; Ponytail and Rust Clean Code keep ownership in the existing helper, and Diagnosing Bugs establishes the failing/green public export loop.

Full locked offline debug tests pass 286 active cases with 44 ignored gates; optimized tests pass 273 active cases with 51 ignored gates. Formatting and strict all-target debug/release Clippy pass. These counts are unchanged because the export extends the existing owner rather than adding a test. Ignored gates and native macOS remain unverified; no real account, paid call, dependency/CI change or commit. The separately activated debug-only saved-file replacement gate passes local stale-account rejection, absent Run and unchanged replay in its private networkless namespace; it is not optimized or native keyring lifecycle evidence.

### Implemented: word editing, newline keys and slash-name acceptance

Inline chat now accepts Ctrl+Left, Ctrl+Right and Ctrl+W through the existing bounded grapheme-aware Composer. Ctrl+Left skips preceding whitespace and reaches the previous chunk start; Ctrl+Right reaches the next chunk start or the draft end; Ctrl+W removes the same backward range. Punctuation stays within a chunk so paths, slash arguments and model IDs remain intact; Unicode whitespace and explicit newlines are separators, and deletion resegments the draft. Ordinary arrows, draft capacity and vertical-column reset retain their existing contracts. The semantic input variants stay in terminal, never Engine or canonical state. Idle chat, active Run/admission and catalog loading route them to the same editor; compaction already delegates draft edits. Open modal pickers, history find and secret fields retain their existing keys.

Verification map: extend the existing key-mapping and Composer corpora with exact modifiers, boundary no-ops, mid-chunk movement, punctuation, Unicode whitespace, combining accents, emoji, multiline text and deletion that joins graphemes. First prove the missing mapping/editor behavior. Extend the existing wrapped-frame owner at 16/24/40/80 columns in color and monochrome for visible text/caret after word edits. Extend the existing isolated Linux tmux idle journey for real keys at 16×8 and its active/maintenance journey for retained edits without submission; preserve their terminal-restoration and closed-replay assertions. No duplicate test function or new framework. Security pass: only authenticated local keyboard input edits the same at-most-8-KiB transient draft; no credential, network, Workspace, Event, retry, new process, queue or dependency is added. Existing selection, cancellation, billing and disclosure authority remain unchanged. Update terminal guidance, setup and the current architecture; consolidate formatting, strict lint, offline suites/build and source/patch review at closeout. Manual/live/native checks and the user-deferred Anthropic route remain final handoff work, not prerequisites for coding.

User additions while this slice is in flight: accept a decoded Shift+Enter as a newline and expose its hint when space permits, preserving Ctrl+O when the terminal sends ordinary Enter for both. Preserve the existing two-second idle empty-draft double-interrupt exit rule, but make its first notice explain Ctrl+Shift+C copying in readable chat feedback. Existing key/frame/linear/tmux owners cover the mapping, hint, exact notice, first-interrupt continuation and restoration. No keyboard-enhancement negotiation or changed active-Run cancellation is implied.

The user also reports that navigating the visible slash-name rows and pressing Enter rejects the prefix rather than using the focused row. While that inline menu is open, Enter will fill the same trusted name/optional-argument space as Tab, without executing it; a later Enter passes the complete draft through ordinary admission. Outside this menu, Enter retains its existing action. Extend the existing Composer completion corpus, bounded menu frames and shipped slash-completion PTY journey to first reproduce that exact rejection, then prove completion followed by explicit submission, unchanged placeholder bytes, local-only feedback, closed replay and terminal restoration. Apply the same rule during active-Run inspection, not during maintenance, which continues to retain every submission. No new registry, I/O, Event or untrusted command source.

### Requested next: account setup and a single in-chat model/effort selector

The user's latest beta 1 decision supersedes the required native/ChatGPT synthetic model-check journey: after account setup, return to ordinary chat with an economical account-visible model and low effort selected; use `/model` for an in-chat list, Up/Down for model focus, Left/Right for that row's effort, and explicit Enter to activate both together. Remove the separate `/effort` command and quick action. Native account choices must not require billable synthetic checks or hide a check behind message submission: the submitted message starts the real bounded Run, whose existing strict response validation still rejects incompatible outcomes. Keep account/consent, credential isolation, TLS, selected destinations, Workspace scope, budgets and no-retry/no-billing-fallback enforcement. Exact custom destination admission retains its distinct security review; it is not permission to add mandatory checks to native model selection.

The new report is a saved ChatGPT account followed by `ChatGPT synthetic conformance failed` for `gpt-5.6-luna`/`low`. That establishes a failed user-observed check, not its cause or a successful Run. Keep it separate from the previous OAuth identity issue, do not rerun paid calls, and do not declare either fixed from offline evidence. Use [official model metadata](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/models/gpt-5.6-luna) and the [selected-account catalog/inference contract](https://developers.openai.com/siwc/token-sharing-open-source/models-and-inference), preserving account-visible IDs and model-supported effort rather than inventing compatibility or global pricing. Implement this next after the current editing slice. The verification map must extend existing account/setup, selector, adapter/admission and exact replay owners to prove no setup inference, no mandatory evidence gate, atomic model/effort activation, Escape/draft/caret/history preservation, busy rejection and strict failure on real incompatible responses. Review canonical provenance honestly rather than forging old conformance evidence. Update current rules, architecture, setup and next steps when the corresponding behavior is implemented.

Current source map: `src/cli/attached/setup.rs` owns catalog-only automatic low-effort selection after account save; `src/cli/attached/models.rs` owns the joint in-chat model/effort picker; `src/cli/attached/controls.rs` shares typed tuple validation and atomic defaults activation; `src/cli/attached/run.rs` revalidates current credential/account admission without mandatory native probes. `src/cli/chatgpt/account.rs` binds current identity/storage consent, not optional diagnostic records. The closed `ChatGptProvenance` record preserves legacy conformance and new account-consent categories for Engine and replay. Catalog visibility and local selection are not capability or credential authority. Admission implementation: retain the closed account/fingerprint record and add a closed admission category. Missing category means legacy conformance and is omitted when re-encoding that category; new Runs explicitly record `account_consent`. This is preferable to two optional fingerprints, which permit ambiguous combinations, or treating an account receipt as a successful probe. The account owner derives a domain-separated, model/effort/identity/storage-consent-bound HMAC and revalidates the selected protected snapshot under the existing account lock after normal token renewal. It neither reads model-check evidence nor performs catalog/inference/Workspace I/O at this boundary. Explicit diagnostic checks remain optional and retain their independent expiring records. Engine and replay validate structure and pin the category, never grant current account authority. Keep the published constructor name, strict streaming decoder, local token acceptance cap, sequential concurrency, and request restrictions unchanged.

Verification: extend existing protected-account corpora to admit without records and across diagnostic expiry, reject changed consent/selection/disconnection/pending replacement, and distinguish admission from check fingerprints. Then verify setup performs catalog discovery only and returns the economic visible default, before implementing the joint in-chat selector. The catalog labels availability without requiring a check. Permission-disabled sign-in and explicit backend/risk consent are unchanged. The economic preference uses official reviewed model facts, not invented catalog pricing or a universal compatibility claim.

Native implementation design: preserve `resolve_native_effort` as reviewed metadata rather than relabeling unknown catalog rows as compatible. Add one shared Run-selection resolver for both native adapters and CLI admission: validate the native profile and bounded model ID, retain reviewed defaults/unsupported-level rejection, and allow an unknown model with an explicit closed effort without evidence or preflight inference. Unknown models retain concurrency one. Ordinary product constructors use this resolver; existing opt-in checked library constructors and CLI diagnostics retain genuine exact-source evidence. This is preferable to opening the metadata resolver, which would advertise embedding or unknown rows as supported, or manufacturing a conformance receipt. Native setup loads only the selected credential's catalog and saves a low-effort default without a picker or probes. Sonnet is the economical reviewed low-effort-capable Anthropic family; the cheaper Haiku 4.5 does not support an effort parameter according to the [official model comparison](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/models/overview), so do not auto-select it with an invalid low-effort request. Implemented joint selector (2026-10-03): a bounded keyboard-only panel overlays lower transcript rows immediately above the retained composer, instead of opening another screen or changing History's viewport/reading anchor. ### Requested: safe text and image paste

Ctrl+Shift+V is normally handled by the terminal emulator. Pasted text must remain an editable draft, including newlines, without an accidental submission. Image paste needs explicit bounded clipboard admission, local attachment presentation, immutable private image data and Provider/Engine multimodal transport and replay; it is not implemented by pasting a filename or saying the shortcut works. Evaluate standard terminal and OS capabilities before desktop-specific adapters, cover Wayland and X11 without a GNOME/KDE requirement, and preserve explicit data-disclosure and cancellation boundaries. Do not inspect the user's clipboard for diagnosis. Design and implement this after the model/setup flow with existing tests where they own each changed claim; defer only native/manual evidence, not the requested functionality.

Explicit clipboard implementation: keep one terminal-owned, cancellable read outside the input reader and Engine. Ctrl+V, or an explicitly encoded Ctrl+Shift+V, starts it only from an editable chat state; `/paste` shares that authority in inline and linear chat. Existing framed emulator paste remains unchanged. The clipboard worker returns one bounded typed payload through a one-slot result, not key events or a submission. On Linux select standard Wayland or local X11, not a desktop product: use a verified installed absolute `wl-paste` or `xclip`, fixed arguments, safe working directory, cleared allowlisted OS transport environment, bounded concurrently drained private pipes, one aggregate two-second deadline and kill/reap ownership. Never search user PATH, accept remote X11, use clipboard OSC, forward Provider credentials or read clipboard content during startup/diagnosis. Refuse an unavailable selected transport rather than switch it silently. Offered types are bounded to 16 KiB; prefer PNG, reject other image-only formats rather than reinterpret them as filenames/text, and otherwise select an approved UTF-8 text target. The terminal keeps edits, Provider progress and signals live while reading; modal entry/release/suspend cancels the owned read. Text admission reuses the atomic eight-KiB Composer path; images use closed PNG admission and the separate count/raw-byte limits. Native client/service and supervisor verification remain distinct from the implemented image flow.

Verification map: extend current key/raw and clipboard lifecycle/input owners with explicit modifier mapping, no implicit activation, malformed/control/over-limit rejection, one outstanding read, interruption, result discard and unchanged draft/caret semantics. Reuse shipped isolated PTY/namespace fixtures for real clipboard-result routing without the user's compositor or clipboard. Native Wayland/X11/macOS service and assistive-technology checks stay at final handoff when unavailable. Security pass: user keyboard/control is the only authority for a local clipboard read; external clipboard data remains untrusted, transient and bounded until explicit submission. No Workspace reads, stored credentials, Provider calls, retries, Event/schema change, process Tool or dependency is introduced by text access. The private terminal clipboard owner earns its depth through two actual OS transports and process lifetime isolation. Update terminal/security/setup guidance and next steps; consolidate local checks after remaining image functionality. No Git operation or commit.

Implemented text access (2026-10-03): explicit Ctrl+V/encoded Ctrl+Shift+V and `/paste` share one terminal-owned worker, existing Composer admission and a one-shot result. `/paste` is a local control, including during a Run, never an objective or Provider input. Modal entry/release/suspend cancel and discard the worker. Linux clients use standard Wayland/local X11, fixed verified paths and an allowlisted cleared environment; concurrent pipe caps, process deadline, cancellation and process-group kill/reap are implemented, not yet fully exercised by a hostile-client owner. The deadline bounds client work, not arbitrary stalled OS filesystem syscalls. Canonical result delivery waits for an empty pending segment, updates its retained byte count and appends a complete inert `Draft (not sent)` preview; explicit later Enter remains necessary. Keyboard/raw, registry, linear preview, active-control and shipped no-transport inline/linear owners pass. The first inline failure assertion used a contiguous phrase that Ratatui splits into cell diffs; the corrected gate waits for the actual error word and fresh final caret. Strict debug lint passes. Source/native client success and supervisor proof remain separate verification debt, not image implementation or a reason to pause the goal.

Image implementation design: keep image bytes as an immutable, bounded semantic value, not a filename, terminal escape or external URL. Initial beta admission supports PNG clipboard data with a complete bounded container/CRC scan, dimensions/pixel bound and no local raster decoding. At most four images and 192 KiB raw image bytes belong to an objective; encoded bytes count toward context and the unchanged 512-KiB wire cap. Canonical `MessageAccepted` v2 stores inline Base64 PNG values under a kind/version-specific 320-KiB payload cap; v1 text encoding and every other 64-KiB Event cap remain unchanged. This avoids a new blob database, mutable paths, cleanup races or orphaned attachments; the trade-off is deliberately small image admission and larger bounded Event records. Store/replay enforce the same exception before parsing. Immutable shared encoded values avoid copying pixels into every RunView snapshot. Standard PNG admission checks structure, not decoded pixel validity; a Provider still validates its actual input.

The Engine owns current attachments and selected complete historical attachments. Context images carry an explicit objective/history-turn origin into native Responses, ChatGPT-plan Responses and Messages content blocks. Omit no image silently within a selected turn; stop history selection when a whole turn exceeds count/byte/context bounds. Child calls receive only current-objective images with their existing isolated assignment context, not historical images. Exact custom profiles remain text-only until image conformance is authorized and implemented; reject selected image input before egress. Keep endpoint, credential, no-retry, outcome, time and output bounds unchanged. Image-only chat can be a user Message without invented text. Draft controls retain attachments; Session switching requires handling them explicitly. Show compact metadata beside the editable draft and in human history, never Base64.

Compaction remains text-and-metadata only: accepted user text, assistant interpretations and bounded immutable image labels/digests, not pixels. Original bytes remain canonical and recoverable in history/forks; after a covered prefix is summarized, later requests use that lossy summary rather than silently restoring covered pixels. Document that trade-off, derive compaction source-pressure headroom from maximal text plus metadata, and preserve strict prefix/content digests. Extend existing Engine journey, wire/parser, Store/reducer, Composer/frame and synthetic product owners for image admission, encoding, exact bytes through closed replay/fork, context association, child isolation, image-only submission, cancellation and over-limit/no-egress failures. Use synthetic PNGs, not the user's clipboard, credentials or paid calls. Run focused checks per changed invariant, then consolidate repository checks. Native service, macOS, assistive-technology and real model support remain final user-owned verification. No dependency, CI change, Git operation or commit.

Provider foundation implemented (2026-10-03): `ImageAttachment` owns bounded immutable shared PNG Base64 and derived dimensions/digest, with the same closed structural admission on construction/deserialization and metadata-only Debug. `ProviderRequest` carries explicit objective/history-turn origins; native API adapters and ChatGPT validate image count/aggregate bytes/source before body construction, and custom admission rejects any image. Responses and Messages emit real inline image blocks, while ChatGPT preserves the resulting message array rather than nesting it into another message. No upload, external image URL, request destination, credential, retry, wire cap or output guarantee changes. Text-only wire documents stay identical. The changed native checker and ChatGPT check/account-admission versions prevent old exact request evidence being silently relabeled; ordinary native Runs still do not require checks, and custom text-only fingerprints remain compatible.

Implemented image flow (2026-10-03): `RunRequest`, Composer, canonical Event/reducer/Store, context, compaction, human history and typed Linux clipboard routing now carry real admitted images. Image-only submission preserves empty text; local controls consume command text but retain attachments, and Session switching rejects pending attachments before effects. Human transcript, linear draft preview and `show` expose only bounded generated metadata; explicit JSONL export retains canonical pixels. Replay bounds image Events before payload access, rejects malformed image collections before a fifth image construction and checks the existing database-size ceiling even in read-only mode. Context accounts for encoded image bytes, clips only whole older turns and keeps children isolated. Image-bearing compaction uses compiler v2 and metadata-aware source headroom; text-only compiler v1 and source/content digests stay compatible.

Focused image evidence: existing wire owners prove exact native image blocks, labels/source association, unchanged strict/output controls, PNG/Base64/control rejection, raw/count/dimension/pixel/animation limits and text-only compatibility. The central Engine journey passes exact semantic root/child/synthesis requests, image-only input, whole-turn clipping, metadata-only compaction, fork lineage and close/reopen byte equality. Its shipped `show` observations distinguish metadata-only human output from exact canonical JSONL; only that parent's export capture increases to 512 KiB, not a product cap. Existing Composer, narrow/color/monochrome frame, linear, active-control and slash/Session owners pass retained attachments, bounded metadata and unchanged text/caret without Base64 display. Text-paste implementation design: retain the public scalar terminal-input vocabulary and the existing Composer, but represent one completed paste with a notification whose bounded payload is taken once from the terminal reader. On Unix, read nonblocking stdin through the already installed Mio/rustix facilities and decode only supported UTF-8 keys, closed CSI/SS3 keyboard forms and picker mouse events. Keep normal escape sequences within 64 bytes, raw reads within 4 KiB, paste content within 8 KiB and the existing 32-event backpressured queue; malformed/oversized paste becomes a whole-record rejection, never a truncated objective or controls. An unterminated record has a ten-second deadline and fails terminal ownership closed rather than replaying its trailing bytes as keys. A lone Escape retains a bounded ambiguity wait. The alternative of enabling Crossterm's paste parser is rejected because the selected parser buffers an unbounded record before application admission; patching that dependency would expand the temporary EOF-only maintenance burden. This private terminal transport earns its boundary through raw-input resource isolation, not a second Engine seam. Keep the local Crossterm patch and dependency features unchanged.

The inline RAII owner enables standard bracketed paste only while its bounded reader owns stdin and disables it on close, suspension, partial failure and unwind. Do not use clipboard OSC, a desktop service, new process or clipboard inspection for terminal-delivered text. Preserve the full-height primary-screen/history contract, deterministic output, selected destinations and account consent. Composer admission normalizes CR/CRLF to LF, retains literal tabs with a safe visual expansion, checks the complete insertion before editing, and resegments the caret after Unicode cluster joins. Paste never submits, completes a slash choice or activates a picker. Route it to the same draft during idle, Run/admission, maintenance and catalog loading; modal selectors ignore it. Hidden API keys and visible workspace IDs need their own atomic, field-bounded admission with no echo or automatic confirmation. Linear/screen-reader mode retains canonical input and no terminal control sequences; its existing explicit Ctrl+D continuation is not proof of safe emulator-delivered multiline paste. The separately implemented explicit cross-mode clipboard and image flow above does not change that framing limitation.

Verification map: extend existing Composer/input corpora for whole-block multiline/tab/Unicode insertion, exact-cap and one-byte-overflow rejection, invalid UTF-8/control bytes, marker fragmentation, pasted command/interrupt bytes, malformed and unterminated records, normal keyboard modifiers and inert unsupported sequences. Extend the existing inline PTY owner for bracketed multiline draft/caret, explicit later submission, hidden-key paste/cancellation, restoration and closed Run-free replay; use synthetic data only. Keep existing pointer, EOF, signal and suspend owners for the changed reader/lifecycle. TestBackend owns visible narrow/color/monochrome draft projection. Record cross-mode clipboard, image, native macOS and theme/assistive-technology gaps precisely; run focused formatting/lint/tests and source/patch/security review before consolidation. Capture the one-shot payload and discard-before-next-input invariant in terminal rules when implemented. No commits, dependency or CI change; full beta goal remains active.

### Completed implementation: verified sign-in without plan permission

The [official ChatGPT recovery contract](https://developers.openai.com/siwc/token-sharing-open-source/errors-and-recovery#chatgpt-plan-use-isnt-enabled) distinguishes a valid signed identity from permission to use the plan: retain verified sign-in without enabling inference when direct permission is absent. The prior code checked plan scopes before verifying identity and discarded that whole result. This was a local functional gap, independent of the user's still-undiagnosed live identity rejection.

Decision: return a closed private sign-in result containing either authorized credentials or verified identity metadata without tokens. Verify signature, issuer, exact client audience, nonce, time and returning subject before admitting the metadata-only branch. Store an explicit permission-disabled account with no tokens or model checks under the existing OS-user account lock and bounds. Reusing only `disconnected` would conflate logout with denied plan permission; the explicit negative-capability marker preserves meaningful recovery and fails closed in older readers. Keep existing enabled records compatible. Setup retains account-only defaults, reports the missing permission and offers explicit enable-plan reauthorization using the same client, host, signed subject and backend. Only that permission-repair choice adds ordinary OAuth `prompt=consent`; routine reauthorization remains unchanged and `force_reconsent` is not admitted. No catalog, conformance, Run, Workspace read or API-billing fallback is allowed for the disabled account. Replacement must block old token use before bounded keyring cleanup; uncertain deletion stays visible without re-enabling the account.

Implement in coherent slices: verified grant classification and protected disabled-account lifecycle, then attached recovery and the existing networkless product journey. Verification map: extend the signed-redemption owner to distinguish absent permission from invalid identity, optional refresh tokens and malformed scope/credential bounds; extend account lifecycle corpora for private metadata-only reopen, exact identity/selection/backend checks, disabled use/evidence rejection, enabled replacement, old-check invalidation and keyring cleanup uncertainty. Reuse setup/authorization URL and product-process owners to prove account-only replay, safe feedback, no inference/catalog egress, explicit repair parameters, successful same-account enablement and draft/terminal restoration. Focused synthetic checks accompany each changed invariant; final format/lint/build and broader consolidation follow implementation. Update ChatGPT/account/setup rules, architecture and next steps with the implemented scope. Security pass preserves compiled endpoints, ordinary TLS, proxy/redirect/retry restrictions, signature checks, bounded transport/storage, explicit backend/cost consent and same-user secrecy limitations. No new dependency, Engine/Event schema, production endpoint override, release infrastructure, Git action, commit, real account or paid call.

### Completed implementation: explicit Anthropic API workspace scope

The current [Anthropic authentication contract](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/manage-claude/authentication#select-a-workspace) requires `anthropic-workspace-id` on every request made with an unscoped personal/service-account key; scoped keys may omit it. This is an API-key compatibility gap, not consumer subscription authority. Bearer authentication remains officially supported and needs no replacement.

Decision: carry a closed native credential value containing the selected Provider, bounded key and optional explicitly selected Anthropic workspace ID. Environment-backed selections read only the selected key and, for Anthropic only, `ANTHROPIC_WORKSPACE_ID`. Direct key-only library calls retain their no-workspace meaning. Catalog pages, conformance calls, Run calls and compaction use the same immutable value; exact native evidence includes the workspace and a bumped checker version. A missing scope never triggers discovery, an Admin API call, fallback or an inferred tenant.

Implement in two coherent slices: (1) typed scope, library/environment transport and evidence admission, preserving existing key-only APIs and protected-account isolation; (2) protected account versioning, visible non-secret workspace selection in setup, and complete saved-account catalog/check/Run plumbing. ### Completed implementation: signed singleton-audience compatibility

The private ID-token schema accepts `aud` as a string or equivalent one-element string array containing only the exact issued client. [OpenAI's sign-in guide](https://developers.openai.com/siwc/token-sharing-open-source/sign-in) requires issued-client audience validation and links its JWT verifier example; it does not promise a particular ID-token audience serialization. [OpenID Connect Core](https://openid.net/specs/openid-connect-core-1_0.html#IDTokenValidation) permits array audiences but requires rejecting untrusted additional audiences and recommends checking a present authorized party. Arany rejects every additional audience rather than adding a trusted-extra-audience registry. A present `azp` must be a non-null string equal to that same client.

Ownership/security: keep normalization private to `identity.rs` and return only the already verified subject. The issuer's signature still precedes claim parsing and all existing issuer, nonce, subject, clock, key, token/body and request limits remain. Bound the audience to one value under the existing claim-byte ceiling, reject empty/non-string/nested or additional audience entries, and never disclose field values in errors. No endpoint, algorithm, egress authority, credential source, account receipt, Run admission, Engine interface, dependency or retry changes. This addresses a standards-backed local compatibility defect, not a proven explanation of the user's live rejection.

Verification map: extend the existing signed-identity corpus with string/singleton-array acceptance, exact-client mismatch and array-shape rejection, present authorized-party match/mismatch/malformed cases, and existing signature/nonce/time failures. Demonstrate the current singleton rejection before the parser change. Extend the existing loopback code-redemption owner to prove only the correctly signed, bound form returns credentials and no token appears in requests/output. Format/lint/build once after source completion; keep complete suites, paid/live, native OS-store and other-machine checks in their established final handoff. Update ChatGPT rules, the identity security review, setup/next steps and this plan with the actual evidence and the still-open live cause.

### Completed audit correction: deterministic Run-ID history inspection

The public contract names `arany show SESSION_OR_RUN_ID`, but the shipped command treated every UUID as a Session ID. The existing custom product-process owner now proves that a committed direct, delegated, or failed Run is inspectable in text and JSONL by its own ID, with exactly that Run's saved Events and the parent Session metadata. A valid missing ID remains a local failure. The existing Session-ID output byte contract is preserved; a Session ID continues to select complete direct Session Events, including any fork reference.

Keep Run-to-Session lookup inside the existing Store owner, reuse its bounded, schema-validating journal scan, retain only one candidate Session ID, and reject conflicting owners. The CLI parses the identifier before State access, opens read-only, validates the parent through strict replay before filtering Events, closes the Store, and uses the existing renderer. ### Completed UI slice: readable conversation and recoverable input

| State | Conversation | Bottom input and status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Idle | Compact welcome near the input; committed turns remain readable | Thin separators frame the editable line; empty-input hint names the next action |
| Typing | Conversation and feedback stay visible | Literal draft and grapheme caret; slash ghosts stay hints; multiline grows upward |
| Working | Committed user turn and stable compact agent activity | Draft remains editable; state/model/context keep their existing priority |
| Picker | Existing named choices with visible focus and activation guidance | Escape restores draft, caret, and reading anchor |
| Error | Sanitized local feedback wraps as a labeled chat entry | Pre-effect account/model rejection preserves the inline draft; status keeps current facts |

**Security and verification map:** protect account tokens, signed identity, consent receipts, Workspace snapshots, and plan usage across browser callback, OS-user credential storage, Provider egress, and durable Events. Current selected-account identity and storage-bound warning consent authorize the subscription route, with explicit model/effort selection; optional diagnostic evidence, browser redirects, catalog rows, other same-user processes and Workspace text are not Run authority. Exact custom destinations retain their separate conformance gate. Prefer the OS keyring for stored subscription tokens with an explicit warning: an unlocked Linux Secret Service item may be read by another same-user app, and Arany cannot promise exclusive access. If no usable keyring exists, require a separate user-confirmed private-file choice and warn that owner-only modes do not encrypt the token or exclude same-user processes. Keep compiled HTTPS endpoints, no proxy/redirect/retry, bounded listener/request/stream/call budgets, no token output or Event persistence, and no fallback to a different billing route. A local stream cap does not bound remote generation. Extend existing synthetic OAuth/account/conformance and product-process Session owners for success, refusal, expiry/rotation, interrupted/failing streams, wrong account/model, and strict replay before a supervised opt-in live turn. Run formatting, strict Clippy, the complete offline suite, dependency policy, a local binary build, and a changed-file review after the final edits; report native macOS, paid live, and redistributable-release checks separately. Record any durable boundary changes in their narrow authoritative document.

## Scope and current state

The one-package executable has a private SQLite Event journal, strict Session/Run/AgentRun replay, pinned Workspace snapshots, bounded direct-child scheduling, cancellation, fork lineage, manual and attached automatic compaction, native OpenAI and Anthropic API adapters, the consented ChatGPT-plan adapter, exact custom profiles, deterministic `exec`/`show`, and an attached terminal with a labeled linear fallback. Native model-specific effort is selectable and pinned; custom capability-evidence v2 profiles declare and conform explicit efforts while v1 remains provider-default only. Catalogs list bounded account-visible native models or the configured custom tuple. Native rows outside the reviewed table require an explicit effort, not synthetic inference; actual Runs retain selected-account admission, strict outcomes and resource bounds. ChatGPT additionally requires current identity and storage-bound consent. Exact custom destinations retain mandatory conformance before Workspace disclosure; optional native/ChatGPT diagnostics do not authorize ordinary Runs. Idle `/model` selects model and effort together in chat, and `/models` remains its alias; `/effort` is retired. The command loop supports Session lifecycle, defaults, progress, retained drafts, signals, `/compact`, and transient mouse selection inside supported pickers. The [requirement audit](#requirement-by-requirement-audit) records each current implementation's scoped evidence and pending checks.

The broader release still needs remaining terminal-fault evidence, broader Linux/macOS terminal and accessibility gates, paid live Provider conformance, and release security and performance gates. Ignored, explicit-opt-in native OpenAI and Anthropic direct/team product-process journeys are compiled but have not been activated or billed. Opt-in local OTLP trace export covers direct/team Runs and compaction through a bounded private module; its disabled, malformed-endpoint, redirect, stalled-Collector, and unavailable-Collector paths have offline evidence, and named-host saturation and healthy-Collector performance gates pass. Historical disabled-path overhead and supply-chain review remain open. Named-host Linux release measurements pass the 100-Event `show` replay, external-runner two-child team, and quiet-host two-file startup gates; a separate core-only Engine result helps distinguish product-process overhead. The monotonic Engine team-cancellation gate previously passed, and the Linux two-file resident-memory gate passes the current release build. Linux PTYs prove SIGTERM/SIGHUP, first- and second-Ctrl+C cancellation, signal during restored output, broken-stderr cancellation, inline raw-mode restoration, linear bounded-draft and inline raw-mode restoration across in-flight suspend, exact cancelled/finished receipts, strict replay, raw-mode restoration after both a cursor-query timeout and a separate stderr write failure during acquisition, and terminal-owner cleanup after a test-helper panic. The explicit Linux tmux gates pass for an attached client and pane with both layers restored, plus a live active-shelf and retained-draft capture. A daemon, nested teams, cross-Session Memory, and built-in broker profiles remain outside beta as specified in the architecture.

Explicitly enabled primary Tools, offline commands, progressive Skills and local stdio MCP are now inside beta 1 through the existing Engine and separate Guard, not another harness. The [coding-base plan](../effectful-beta-base/README.md) owns implementation/evidence and [tool guide](../../docs/tools.md) owns grants, Linux enforcement prerequisites, bounds and unsupported capabilities. Default Runs and direct children remain read-only; real tool-capable Provider compatibility remains a final user check.

The [interactive PTY-loss finding](../../docs/security/findings/terminal-pty-hangup-spin.md) is repaired for the tested Linux path: with user approval, a checksum-matched local Crossterm 0.29.0 patch now propagates terminal EOF and permanent read errors. All three default-feature Linux PTY-loss regressions and the complete offline suite pass without the temporary alternate backend or a detached reader. A checked-in source-archive script preserves the relative patch and verifies a fresh extracted offline build; ordinary Cargo `.crate` packaging strips it and is not a fixed installation path. Native macOS PTY-loss, exact source/binary distribution pairing, and complete dependency/host provenance remain release gates.

The PTY-loss test guard recognizes the exact test binary even when Linux appends ` (deleted)` after a rebuild, while still requiring the private State argument before signaling. The host reboot removed the 45 previously observed orphan processes; none was signaled. The default-feature regressions now pass with the local dependency repair, but native macOS proof remains open.

Explicit Linux kernel file-growth gates now prove that the Store rejects a failed append without losing its committed prefix and that the shipped `exec` process reports the failure without an answer or Provider egress. Both recover after the limit ends. Private-tmpfs gates cover actual Linux `ENOSPC` at both the Store boundary and the shipped `exec` process. Block-device exhaustion, power loss, and native macOS remain unverified.

The added beta scope includes slash previews, Tab/Enter name completion with persistent argument placeholders, protected OS-user API accounts and an explicitly consented ChatGPT subscription route. An unconfigured start keeps the ordinary composer available and gives local `/setup` guidance rather than forcing setup. Native and ChatGPT setup read only the selected account catalog, save an economical visible low-effort choice and return to chat without inference or additional model/effort/cost questions. The optimized networkless ChatGPT journey proves signed synthetic login and durable direct/team turns without mandatory checks, not real account compatibility. ChatGPT's local-only output bound remains weaker than API-key routes. Anthropic consumer subscription integration remains user-deferred missing functionality, separately from final manual verification in [next steps](../../NEXT_STEPS.md).

The user also requested restrained terminal polish as the **last** beta slice: a chat-first hierarchy, bottom multiline composer and status, compact nearby agent activity, visible focus and completion, and preservation of draft and reading context. The [UI work](#in-progress-final-slice-attached-terminal-visual-and-interaction-polish) now uses a full-height primary-screen transcript with a fixed-bottom borderless composer, `PageUp`/`PageDown` history, bounded literal `Ctrl+F` find, and `Ctrl+L` live-tail return; it does not enter alternate screen. An empty Session has a short welcome; conditional agent activity sits above the composer, with status below. The status prioritizes model and the current Run's recorded context-input bytes when space permits; active `/status` discloses those bytes at narrow widths. A confirmed `You:`/`Arany:` transcript is reconstructed from Session history, while the existing `Answer:` receipt still emits after restoration. Semantic accents with `--no-color`/`NO_COLOR` work; native macOS, manual terminal-theme/accessibility review, and further historical-navigation fault evidence remain open.

The [beta dependency inventory](../../docs/security/beta-dependency-inventory.md) records the locked and selected Linux and both macOS target graphs, build-time and native-code review surfaces, license declarations, and missing native-platform evidence. Ratatui/Crossterm were upgraded together; the prior three RustSec warnings are absent from a freshly fetched, strict whole-lockfile audit, but the migration expands the lockfile and invalidates complete-entrypoint-review claims until the new graph is reconciled. The current `cargo-deny` policy passes with a version-specific `option-ext 0.2.0` MPL-2.0 exception; the point-in-time advisory scan passed on 2026-10-02. The Zbus macro path invoked the `CARGO`-selected executable twice in a controlled offline Linux `secret-service` package build; a separate compiler probe observed `/usr/bin/cargo` despite a hostile ambient override. Exact release-host executable provenance remains open alongside the `libc` fixed-name probe. A point-in-time scan is not permanent advisory, expanded-code, legal, or artifact clearance. The current verified package list includes both `LICENSE` and `NOTICE`. The [SQLite](../../docs/security/findings/sqlite-build-environment-override.md), [AWS-LC](../../docs/security/findings/aws-lc-system-autodetection.md), [ICU4X](../../docs/security/findings/icu4x-external-data-source.md), [libc fixed-name executable](../../docs/security/findings/libc-build-path-executable.md), and [native compiler-input](../../docs/security/findings/native-compiler-environment-inputs.md) findings remain open in their release scopes. The guard now rejects the known ambient `CC`/`CFLAGS` families, while compiler identity, other build inputs, and native macOS proof remain open. The [macOS TLS decision](../../docs/security/findings/macos-tls-platform-network-fetch.md) accepts standard OS certificate-related traffic without granting it Arany payload authority; native behavior remains unverified. The [native license/notice research](../../docs/security/beta-native-license-notice-research.md) adds one Linux build observation but does not clear redistribution.

The [decision register](../../docs/research/next-step-decision-register.md) resolves product behavior, budgets, and major seams. [Testing](../../agents/testing.md), [security](../../agents/security.md), [sessions](../../agents/session.md), [providers](../../agents/provider.md), and [terminal](../../agents/terminal.md) supply the working contracts.

### Completed slice: active terminal history ownership

An attached Run previously cloned the whole resolved `SessionView` before polling progress and input. Near the Session history cap, this copied tens of megabytes before the first active redraw. Keep one terminal-local view owned by the attached loop; update its committed Run projection in place, retaining only the selected Provider and model strings for the pinned Run future. On completion or an error, the existing path reloads the canonical committed view. No Event, Provider request, Workspace input, terminal ownership, or history-navigation contract changes.

Verification map: check both attached submission paths and the completion/error reload paths, then run formatting, strict Clippy, the complete offline suite, the explicit Linux tmux active-Run/history/draft gate, an extracted-source build, and changed-source review. Measure the near-cap active keyboard through the shipped executable in a private no-color tmux pane and verify cancellation, draft retention, terminal settings, and read-only replay; report debug and optimized results separately. Native macOS and manual accessibility evidence remain open. No commit during this goal.

Result: both attached submission paths use the caller-owned view, and active progress updates only the current Run projection. Formatting, strict Clippy, the full offline suite, the Linux tmux active-Run gate, the extracted-source build, and that slice's Linux candidate bundle pass; all 35 bundled member hashes verified after extraction. A new opt-in Linux tmux test seeds 1,600 completed Runs and 65,528,000 transcript bytes, then submits one held synthetic Run and measures ten visible draft keys while navigating history. It proves cancellation, retained draft, exact terminal-setting restoration, and strict read-only replay with no second submission. One debug observation took 4.82 s from Enter to Provider request and had 16.0 ms key p50/16.6 ms maximum; the optimized product took 410 ms and 3.8 ms key p50/5.1 ms maximum. These host-specific timings include tmux command and capture overhead, are not a continuous latency guarantee, and do not isolate each admission phase. The candidate is not redistribution-approved: `cargo-deny` still rejects the unapproved `option-ext` MPL-2.0 license. No commit was made.

The repaired gate passed with one debug sample (4.68 s admission; 14.9 ms key p50, 15.8 ms maximum) and three optimized samples (309–398 ms admission; 3.29–3.40 ms key p50, 4.80–5.50 ms maximum). The host had 16 logical CPUs, load average 1.08, and CPU pressure `avg10=0.00` before the optimized series. These samples include tmux command/capture overhead and prove neither a continuous latency bound, native macOS behavior, nor arbitrary near-cap Event mixes. The test uses only private synthetic State and a held loopback Provider; no product authority or production source changed.

After the reading-status refinement, another debug/release pair passed at 4.74 s/371 ms admission, 14.39/3.36 ms key p50, and 16.66/4.81 ms key maximum. The 1,600-Run, 65,528,000-byte corpus and the scope limits above are unchanged.

### Completed slice: native model-catalog media type

The native OpenAI and Anthropic catalog transport currently decodes a bounded JSON body without checking its declared media type. Keep the existing Provider module and fixture owner; reject a missing, duplicate, or non-JSON `Content-Type` before reading catalog bytes. Accept a single `application/json` type with optional parameters, as the separate ChatGPT-plan catalog already does. Parsing a body regardless of its type would be smaller but would weaken the selected response contract; this is a fail-closed transport policy, not a claim that every upstream response has been observed live.

Security pass: the selected account key and model catalog are protected. The remote response header is untrusted data; it cannot authorize a model, effort, endpoint, or Workspace disclosure. A wrong media type fails as `InvalidOutcome` before body allocation, without retry, credential reflection, or new network traffic. The existing 1 MiB/page, 16-page, and 4,096-model limits remain unchanged.

Verification map: extend the existing loopback catalog corpus for both native profiles with missing, wrong, duplicate, and parameterized JSON media types; first observe the current acceptance of a missing type, then check the fail-closed result. Preserve the existing pagination, encoding, and model-admission owners. Update Provider and architecture contracts, run formatting, strict Clippy, the full offline suite and package verification, review the touched source, and perform auto-learning. Paid live Providers and native macOS remain separate gates. No commit during this goal.

Result: the existing loopback corpus first showed OpenAI accepting JSON with no media type. The shared native catalog transport now requires exactly one `application/json` header (optional parameters) before body reading; the corpus rejects absent, HTML, and duplicate headers for both native profiles while retaining parameterized JSON. Model eligibility, key selection, endpoints, and wire requests are unchanged. Formatting, strict Clippy, the full offline suite, and offline package verification pass. Native live catalogs and macOS remain unverified; no commit was made.

### Completed slice: guarded native release build

Add the user-approved checked-in guard for AWS-LC, SQLite, and ICU4X to a fresh native release build. It rejects ambient source-selection and SQLite compile-option variables before Cargo, pins bundled AWS-LC and SQLite selection, rejects external ICU4X data, and checks the resulting build records and dynamic linkage. This is a release entry point, not a general claim that arbitrary direct `cargo build` invocations are guarded. Keep native compiler, SDK, PATH, `libc` fixed-name executable, advisory, notice, and native macOS review separate.

Security pass: the protected assets are published source provenance, SQLite-backed canonical history, URL/IDNA parsing, and TLS cryptography. Build-host environment is the attacker-controlled input; a Workspace or Provider response cannot set it. The guard must fail before build when any named override is present, even when `ICU4X_DATA_DIR` is empty. Fresh build records must prove the bundled paths, and final linkage must not silently substitute a system library. A successful local Linux build does not attest compiler identity, all native source bytes, or macOS artifacts.

Verification map: use one table-driven process test for hostile variables and a shell syntax check; run the real fresh guarded Linux build, inspect its selected build outputs and final binary, and retain macOS as a native release gate. Run formatting, strict Clippy, full offline tests, offline package verification, changed-file review, and auto-learning. No commit during this goal.

Result: the [checked-in guard](../../scripts/release-build.sh) runs a fresh offline locked release build on a supported native host, rejects the three ambient override families before Cargo, and checks AWS-LC, SQLite, ICU4X, and binary-linkage evidence. Its table-driven hostile-environment test, shell syntax check, and fresh Linux release build pass. Formatting, strict Clippy, the full offline suite, and package verification pass; the package includes the guard. The guard is a required release entry point, not protection for direct Cargo invocations. Both native macOS builds, compiler/PATH/SDK provenance, the separate `libc` executable candidate, advisory/license gates, paid Providers, and the Rust 1.88 MSRV check remain open. No commit was made.

### Completed slice: native model effort selection

Add an explicit, typed reasoning-effort choice for the currently admitted native OpenAI and Anthropic models. The Session stores an optional next-Run choice; native admission resolves an omitted choice to the reviewed model default and sends that value explicitly on every Run and compaction request. `RunStarted` records the resolved value; legacy records remain readable. `exec --effort`, attached `--effort`, and idle `/effort` are user controls. Changing Provider or model clears an incompatible prior choice. Custom exact profiles remain at their already-conformed default until a future evidence version binds an explicit effort; this is an open part of the broader request, not a completed custom feature.

Security pass: effort changes cost and output behavior, not authority. Only an authenticated CLI/Session choice can set it; Workspace and Provider content cannot. Validate the selected value against the reviewed model before reading Workspace input or making an inference request. Preserve the aggregate Run token cap, retry policy, endpoint, credential, and strict outcome encoding. An active Run stays pinned even if a draft contains `/effort`.

Verification map: add model/effort rows to existing native wire fixtures, Session defaults/replay and command-registry owners, and a product-process rejection case that proves no state or Provider request for an unsupported combination. Verify direct and compaction request bodies carry the same pinned effort; older Event JSON without the field still replays. Run formatting, strict Clippy, the full offline suite, package verification, changed-file review, and auto-learning. Paid live checks and native macOS remain separate. No commit during this goal.

Result: native OpenAI and Anthropic effort choices are model-specific, explicit on the wire, and pinned in durable Run config. `exec --effort`, attached `--effort`, and idle `/effort` select the next Run; unsupported choices fail before Workspace input. Focused fixtures, legacy replay, formatting, strict Clippy, the full offline suite, and package verification pass. Live conformance and native macOS remain open. No commit was made.

Allow an exact `openai-responses` custom profile to declare a bounded, ordered effort set under capability-evidence version 2. Preserve v1 fingerprints and replay. Compare profile-fixed effort with a selectable set: a fixed effort reduces probes but cannot satisfy between-Run choice; a set needs one additional synthetic strict finish per value but keeps one exact endpoint/model identity. Use the set, with the three existing default-behavior probes retained. Keep protocol complexity private to the existing custom Provider files rather than adding a crate or Engine seam.
