Only the trusted state-root profile and explicit CLI/Session choice are authority; neither Workspace text nor Provider output can add an effort. Validate declared choices before reading the key, invalidate evidence when the effort set changes, re-resolve the pinned destination, and enforce the same remote output cap, strict schema, no-proxy/no-redirect/no-retry behavior, and 180-second total check deadline. At most six effort probes are added to the three baseline probes. Synthetic loopback evidence proves local admission and wire behavior, not arbitrary remote model support or a paid live claim.

Verification map: extend the closed-profile table for v1/v2 and invalid/duplicate/order cases; extend the existing conformance fixture to inspect baseline and each effort request; use one shipped-process loopback journey for an undeclared-effort rejection, a selected-effort Run, exact output channels, and reopened strict replay. Reuse native wire fixtures for the shared Responses effort document, then run formatting, strict Clippy, the full offline suite, package verification, changed-file review, and auto-learning. Paid live and native macOS remain separate gates. No commit during this goal.

Result: v2 profiles with declared efforts can pass synthetic conformance; `exec --effort` and idle `/effort` select only a declared value, and Run admission pins it on Provider calls and durable replay. v1 remains provider-default only. Synthetic unit and product-process tests, formatting, strict Clippy, the full offline suite, and package verification pass. No paid call or commit was made.

### In-progress slice: provider model catalog and broader model selection

Next implementation seam for unreviewed native models: add an explicit, potentially billable `provider check` action for one account-visible Provider/model/effort tuple. It must use only synthetic prompts and the existing strict native request/decoder paths, prove direct finish, one-child delegation, and compaction under finite remote output caps, and persist bounded, expiring evidence tied to the exact adapter/protocol/model/effort and selected credential identity without storing the credential. Once State opens, a recheck clears stale evidence before probes. `exec`, setup, and idle `/models` may admit that tuple only after revalidating evidence before Workspace input; listing, typing, and ordinary selection never trigger a paid probe. A model lacking support for strict outcomes or the selected effort stays visible but unrunnable. The checked native constructor must verify evidence itself rather than accepting a caller-supplied boolean, while the existing reviewed constructors remain closed to exact compiled IDs. The check must disclose a bounded maximum call/token count and require an affirmative cost action. No native conformance call will run in this environment before the user supplies keys. Schema version 3 supplies a separate 256-row, 24-hour native-evidence Store interface with exact opaque-fingerprint lookup and fail-closed parsing.

Environment-key evidence is HMAC-bound to the exact key and an `env` source marker. Saved-account evidence is bound to that key, its UUID, and a `saved` marker; no raw key enters SQLite. The global OS-account slot scope decision remains open. The custom evidence table remains separate at 32 rows. [Official OpenAI model retrieval](https://developers.openai.com/api/reference/cli/resources/models/methods/retrieve) returns identity/ownership metadata but no strict-output or effort capability fields; [Structured Outputs](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/structured-outputs) and [reasoning effort](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/reasoning) are model-dependent. [Anthropic model metadata](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/api/models/retrieve) can expose structured-output and effort support, but catalog claims alone still do not prove Arany's exact outcome decoder and team/compaction behavior. These are source-backed capability distinctions, not live conformance evidence.

Security pass for this seam: the assets are the selected key, Workspace bytes, Session history, and bounded user spend. Explicit user invocation is the only authority to incur the synthetic cost. Model IDs/capabilities from catalogs and responses are untrusted data, not admission; evidence must bind to a credential identity that cannot be swapped silently, expire, and be invalidated when the adapter contract changes. Three bounded requests for one tuple are the maximum for one check, with zero retry, no Workspace text, fixed native endpoint, no proxy/redirect, and existing response-body limits. A successful offline loopback or one synthetic paid check is not a blanket claim about every returned model or future Provider behavior.

Verification map for this seam: an offline boundary corpus must prove exact tuple validation, explicit cost consent, no network on listing/typing/failed preflight, bounded direct/delegate/compact request and response semantics, and evidence invalidation on tuple, credential, adapter, or expiry change. A Store migration/reopen scenario must prove old journals remain readable and malformed evidence fails closed. A shipped-process scenario must verify that an unreviewed model cannot receive Workspace data before current evidence, and that successful evidence pins model/effort and provenance through Run and replay. Paid live checks require separately supplied keys and opt-in on each claimed native Provider/model tuple; native macOS remains a separate gate. Finish with formatting, strict Clippy, offline tests, package verification, changed-file review, and auto-learning. No commit.

Storage result: schema version 3 adds a separate native-evidence table without altering canonical Events or custom evidence. The Store accepts only a 32-byte opaque fingerprint, enforces a 24-hour lifetime and 256-row limit, and fails closed on malformed rows; read-only old journals stay readable. Focused reopen/expiry/capacity tests and v1/v2 migration tests pass. The checker now consumes the Store interface, so its temporary unused-interface annotations were removed. No paid call or commit was made.

Native-check result: `provider check openai|anthropic MODEL --effort LEVEL --accept-cost` validates consent and a bounded model ID before State creation, reads only the selected environment key, confirms account-catalog visibility, and runs at most three strict synthetic native calls with 1,024 remote output tokens each under one 180-second deadline. `--saved-account` instead selects the current OS-stored API account and binds evidence to its UUID. The exact HMAC-SHA256 fingerprint binds protocol/endpoint, Provider, model, effort, package/check version, key, and environment-versus-saved-account identity; no key enters SQLite. Checked native constructors reload evidence before an environment-backed `exec` or attached saved-account Run can read Workspace data and pin unproven model concurrency to one, while reviewed constructors remain closed to their exact table. Idle attached `/model` or `/models` can stage an unreviewed model ID; `/effort` selects an evidenced tuple. The later setup flow uses a separate consented check bound to its provisional saved-account UUID. Offline tests cover consent/preflight order, probe semantics, exact evidence reopening and mismatches, Store age/capacity, and old-schema migrations. This does not prove live Provider support, model quality, actual effort behavior, or absolute secret protection; the HMAC relies on high-entropy Provider keys and Session history remains unencrypted. Live paid gates, the global account-slot decision, full setup/save/reuse evidence, and native macOS remain open. No paid call or commit was made.

For every configured Provider, expose its full available model list and let the user select a model and an effort before a Run. Native OpenAI and Anthropic have account-scoped Models APIs; a custom exact profile can expose only its configured model unless a separately admitted catalog protocol is designed. Distinguish catalog availability from Arany's strict-outcome compatibility, mark unrunnable entries explicitly, and keep exact custom conformance tied to model and endpoint. OpenAI's model-list object does not carry per-model effort/strict-output capabilities; Anthropic's current object does. Capability evidence and user-visible eligibility must therefore be separate from raw discovery. Show every account-visible catalog model with an unsupported label when it lacks Arany evidence; user feedback may refine this presentation choice.

Security pass: listing reads only the selected Provider credential and sends no Workspace data; model IDs and display names are untrusted, bounded presentation data. Discovery cannot authorize a Run or bypass exact custom evidence. Model and effort must be pinned before Workspace input and `RunStarted`, never changed mid-Run; unsupported combinations fail before egress. Catalog calls need the existing no-proxy/no-redirect/no-retry, timeout, identity-encoding, and response-size rules, with finite pagination and overflow failure.

Verification map: use hand-reviewed offline catalog fixtures with pagination, malformed/oversized responses, hostile text, credential isolation, and unavailable API outcomes. Extend the existing command-registry and Session process owner for selection, pinned replay, between-Run switching, and locked active-Run behavior; exercise provider-specific effort request encoding and rejection with existing wire-fixture owners. Paid live compatibility and native macOS remain explicit gates. Update Provider, CLI, Session, architecture, and user-facing help docs with the implemented contract, then run formatting, strict Clippy, full offline tests, package verification, diff review, and auto-learning. No commit during this goal.

Progress: the read-only `provider models` command now lists OpenAI and Anthropic account catalogs completely within explicit page/count/byte bounds, or one exact configured custom model and its declared efforts without egress. Offline loopback fixtures cover native authentication, Anthropic pagination, hostile data, duplicate IDs, response encoding, and count overflow; the product-process custom listing requires no key. Idle `/model` rejects unreviewed native IDs and points to the catalog. Custom explicit effort is available only through v2 exact-profile conformance; broader native model admission still needs reviewed capabilities and live evidence. Paid live catalog and inference checks have not run. No commit was made.

Completed slice: extend the reviewed native model/effort table to the current API text-and-structured-output lineups with documented effort support. OpenAI's `gpt-6-astra`, `gpt-6.1-sol`, and `gpt-6-luna` use the existing strict Responses adapter; Anthropic's `claude-fable-5-1`, `claude-opus-5-5`, and `claude-sonnet-5-5` use the existing strict Messages adapter. Compare admitting every catalog ID directly with a reviewed table: raw availability does not prove strict outcome or effort compatibility, so retain the table and list other IDs as unsupported. This is a finite near-term expansion, not the still-needed account-scoped admission path for future compatible models. The user approved an explicit, potentially billable synthetic conformance check for a catalog model lacking reviewed compatibility evidence. It must remain a deliberate action before that exact model becomes runnable, never an automatic side effect of listing, typing, or selecting it. No paid check has run.

Security pass: the selected account, key, endpoint, output caps, no-proxy/no-redirect/no-retry client, and strict response parser remain unchanged. A newly reviewed model/effort pair is still validated before reading its key or Workspace input, and only the exact selected model reaches the Provider. Catalog text is not authority; unlisted models and unsupported effort values continue to fail closed. Offline fixtures establish request and decoder shape, not live conformance or a model-quality claim. The opt-in paid direct/team product-process gate must cover each newly claimed model before release.

Verification map: extend the existing model/effort admission table with default, allowed, and rejected combinations; reuse both adapters' wire-fixture owners to check that newly selected IDs and efforts are encoded and decoded without relaxing strict outcomes; extend a catalog row to show reviewed versus unsupported entries. Keep product-process rejection and Run-pinning owners unchanged unless their claim changes. Update Provider and architecture documentation and the beta evidence register, then run formatting, strict Clippy, full offline tests, offline package verification, changed-file review, and auto-learning. No commit during this goal.

Result: the reviewed table now admits four exact models per native Provider. Existing wire fixtures cover new model IDs, effort encoding, strict-schema preservation, and exact response-model rejection; a shipped-process missing-key case confirms new model/effort admission occurs before State or Workspace use. Catalog fixtures distinguish the added rows from unsupported entries. The ignored paid live direct/team journey now requires an explicit `ARANY_LIVE_MODEL`, so each release claim can be checked separately without an implicit model choice. Formatting, strict Clippy, the full offline suite, and offline package verification pass. No paid calls, macOS checks, or commit were made; arbitrary future account-visible models remain unsupported pending a separate admission design and evidence.

### Completed slice: native credential-reflection rejection

Reject a selected native API key if it appears in a raw OpenAI or Anthropic inference response, or in a decoded Run outcome, response ID, or compaction summary. This closes a gap with the custom adapter before broadening native model admission. Keep the fixed native endpoints and existing Provider interface; use one private semantic check on the already bounded response values rather than adding a new transport seam. A rejected reply produces the existing typed invalid-outcome failure and no committed answer or compaction summary.

Security pass: the protected asset is the selected API key, whether environment-backed or OS-store-backed. The direct Provider reply is untrusted input even though destination and TLS are pinned. Only the selected account/key authorizes its Provider call; a returned string cannot authorize disclosure or become trusted because a schema was followed. Scan the bounded raw reply before decoding and the decoded fields before returning them to Engine, including JSON-escaped echoes; never include the key in errors, Events, telemetry, or test diagnostics. The existing 1 MiB response, token, time, no-proxy, no-redirect, and no-retry limits stay unchanged. This is exact-key reflection protection, not a general secret-detection or compromised-Provider prevention claim.

Verification map: extend each native adapter's existing wire owner with a valid response and a decoded escaped-key rejection for both Run and compaction. A compact shared semantic corpus covers finish, delegation, IDs, and raw bytes; the custom product-process reflection owner remains the durability/channel proof for the same policy, without inventing a native test endpoint. Review the existing canary boundaries, update Provider rules and architecture only where the contract changes, then run formatting, strict Clippy, full offline tests, offline package verification, changed-file review, and auto-learning. Paid live checks and native macOS remain separate gates. No commit during this goal.

Result: both native adapters now reject exact selected-key bytes before wire decoding and reject a JSON-escaped echo after decoding Run or compaction fields. Custom transport uses the same private bounded-byte and semantic checks. Synthetic adapter cases pass for valid replies and escaped reflections; the shared corpus covers raw bytes, finish/delegation values, response IDs, and compaction. A repeated screen-reader model-picker PTY exposed a test synchronization race: sending the choice after the header let canonical echo split a row. Waiting for the complete choice prompt eliminated the reproduced failure in 100 targeted runs. Formatting, strict Clippy, full offline tests, and offline package verification pass. This is not paid live or native macOS evidence. No commit was made.

### Completed slice: durable Provider-call accounting

Normal Run responses currently carry a bounded response ID and reported input/output tokens into optional telemetry, but `AgentFinished` discards them from canonical history. Persist one typed call-result fact after each completed Engine invocation and before its AgentRun terminal fact, including primary planning, every started child, and synthesis. The record carries phase, locally observed disposition, accepted response ID, and reported usage when available; unknown usage remains unknown after timeout, cancellation, or transport failure. A cancelled call fact does not assert that a remote request was sent or that billing stopped. `RunStarted` remains the immutable selected profile/model/effort authority. Compare embedding a call list in `AgentFinished` with a separate Event: the embedded list saves an append but loses already observed calls if the process dies before the AgentRun finishes; choose the separate Event so a valid interrupted prefix retains known usage. Keep Provider wire objects and terminal geometry outside Engine/Session, and do not add another behavior seam or dependency.

Security pass: protected assets are canonical cost/provenance facts, selected credentials, Workspace content, and the finite Run budget. The selected Provider and `RunStarted` authorize a call; its reply is untrusted data, never authority. Admit only bounded printable response IDs and counts within the pinned cap, reject malformed Event scopes/transitions during replay, and emit no raw body, key, prompt, or arbitrary header. At most `N + 2` call records belong to a successful team Run; a failed or cancelled Run can have fewer, and a Store failure leaves an interrupted prefix. Children retain read-only direct-child authority; this change adds no egress, retries, Tools, or new credential source. Existing OS/Provider endpoint enforcement is unchanged. Local counts are provider-reported observations, not an independently verified bill or guarantee against remote cost after an interrupted call.

Verification map: extend the scripted central direct/team/cancellation journey to compare each persisted call fact with strict reopened replay and JSONL, including reverse child completion order and absent usage on an interrupted call. Add malformed phase/order, duplicate, over-cap, and hostile response-ID cases to the existing Event/reducer corpus. Keep adapter wire fixtures as owners of native reported usage and exact-key reflection; do not duplicate their HTTP parsing. Update architecture, Provider/Session/Engine/Store rules and any exact output goldens intentionally, then run formatting, strict Clippy, full offline tests, package verification, changed-source review, and auto-learning. Paid live usage, per-adapter finish reason/privacy provenance, native macOS, and release performance remain separately unproved. No commit during this goal.

Result: `ProviderCallRecorded` v1 now persists each observed Run-call phase, local disposition, bounded response ID, and provider-reported usage before the AgentRun's terminal Event. A partial journal retains known call facts while replay marks the Run interrupted; older journals without them still replay. Direct/team, reverse-order children, cancellation with unknown usage, strict JSONL, malformed order/duplicate, and payload bounds have offline tests. OTLP exports only the Event kind and sequence marker, not the call payload. Formatting, strict Clippy, the full offline suite (194 passed, 30 intentionally ignored), and offline package verification pass. Paid usage accuracy and native macOS remain open. No commit was made.

### Completed slice: typed Provider wire provenance

Persist the accepted wire completion condition, reported-usage basis, and locally requested storage control for Run and compaction responses. The three admitted adapters use two concrete wire families: Responses `status: completed` and Messages `stop_reason: end_turn`. A `store: false` Responses request records only that Arany requested no response-application-state storage; it does not assert account-level Zero Data Retention, absence of abuse-monitoring logs, or compliance by an exact custom endpoint, as distinguished in [OpenAI's data controls](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/your-data). Anthropic's [Messages stop-reason contract](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/handling-stop-reasons) has no equivalent local storage switch in Arany's request and records no such claim. Scripted/legacy responses may have unknown provenance. Compare having Engine infer provenance from a Provider name with carrying a small typed fact from each adapter: inference leaks wire policy into Engine and can mislabel a custom endpoint, so carry the accepted fact through the existing Provider result seam. Do not add a transport trait, module, dependency, paid call, or output field merely to display it.

Security pass: canonical history and privacy/cost claims are protected assets; Provider bytes, including a custom endpoint's replies, are untrusted. The adapter's reviewed request builder is the authority for a local request-control claim, while its strict decoder is the authority for accepted completion and usage basis; neither is proof of remote retention or billing. Use closed enums with legacy-optional Event fields, reject contradictory or malformed provenance at the Event boundary, and keep secrets, raw bodies, and uncontrolled headers out of Events/telemetry. Timeouts and rejected responses retain unknown wire provenance. Make compaction response IDs obey the same bounded printable-ASCII admission as Run IDs. Existing no-proxy, no-redirect, no-retry, body, token, and deadline limits stay fixed.

Verification map: the existing OpenAI/Anthropic wire fixture owners assert the typed accepted completion, usage basis, and storage-request claim for Run and compaction; the custom product-process journey confirms Responses provenance survives closed-Store replay without promoting the endpoint to a native privacy guarantee. Extend the existing direct/team/compaction scripted journey for optional legacy fields and malformed combinations, and add one hostile compaction-ID row to the current bound corpus. Review exact JSONL, telemetry exclusions, and architecture/Provider/Session/Engine/Store rules; then run formatting, strict Clippy, full offline tests, package verification, changed-source review, and auto-learning. Paid live, remote data-retention verification, and native macOS remain separate release gates. No commit during this goal.

Result: `ProviderWireProvenance` now carries a closed, adapter-owned accepted wire condition and local storage-request classification through Run-call and compaction Events. Old payloads omit it and still replay. OpenAI/Anthropic Run and compaction fixtures assert both variants; the custom product-process direct/team path asserts closed-Store replay, while rejected reflected-key responses retain no accepted provenance. A malformed provenance/usage combination fails Event validation, and a control-bearing compaction response ID becomes a durable typed failure without persisting that ID. The marker-only OTLP export and exact scripted JSONL remain unchanged. Formatting, strict Clippy, the full offline suite (194 passed, 30 intentionally ignored), focused custom process replay, and offline package verification pass. This records Arany's request and accepted response, not remote retention, account-level privacy, or bill accuracy. Paid live and native macOS remain open. No commit was made.

### In-progress slice: slash-command completion and argument previews

The inline composer must show bounded previews for the closed `/` command registry. Tab completes a unique command prefix and, where a trusted finite choice exists, an argument choice. A command with an unfilled argument keeps a visibly distinct placeholder at that position until the user types a nonempty value; the placeholder is presentation state, never part of the draft, submitted command, Run objective, or Event. Ambiguous prefixes show choices without silently selecting or executing one. Future Skills may reuse this interaction only after their separate execution/authority design; they do not register beta slash commands.

Compare a parser-owned command/argument schema with renderer-specific hints: a schema keeps completion, usage, and preview in agreement across terminal modes; renderer-specific strings are smaller initially but duplicate the command contract. Use one private trusted schema behind the existing terminal command registry, with a bounded suggestion count and no catalog/network access on each keystroke. Inline mode owns Tab and ghost placeholders; linear/screen-reader mode keeps canonical input and uses labeled append-only usage/choice hints rather than raw-mode completion. User-entered text is the only executable value. A preview cannot mutate Session defaults or start a Run.

Security pass: protect command authority, draft fidelity, terminal state, and objective privacy. Registry metadata is compiled local authority; Workspace, Provider, model IDs, Session history, and future Skill text are untrusted input. Sanitize and bound any displayed argument candidates, never interpret a ghost placeholder as input, and never perform Provider discovery or key lookup merely because `/` was typed. Completion must preserve the 8 KiB draft bound, exact parser semantics, `//` escape, active-Run locks, and inert terminal output. No new Tool or remote egress is authorized.

Verification map: command-registry tables own exact/ambiguous/unknown prefixes and argument placeholders; composer tests own Tab insertion, grapheme cursor movement, editing, and capacity; Ratatui TestBackend owns inline ghost placement at narrow widths and monochrome semantics; linear fixtures own labeled non-redrawing hints. A Linux PTY product-process test must prove that Tab completion leaves the placeholder visible until an argument is typed, Enter submits only real draft bytes, and quitting restores terminal modes. Native macOS and manual VoiceOver/Orca review remain release gates. Update the terminal contract and help text with implemented behavior, then run formatting, strict Clippy, offline tests, package verification, changed-file review, and auto-learning. No commit during this goal.

Progress: the existing trusted command registry owns argument placeholders and finite static choices. Inline Tab completes a unique slash-command prefix or Provider/collaboration value; ambiguous names remain unchanged. An explicit `/models` browse now caches only selectable model IDs for Tab, invalidates them on Provider change, and offers effort candidates for the selected reviewed native model or loaded exact custom profile. Unsupported catalog rows never become executable suggestions. The inline frame renders a dim ghost separately from real draft bytes and restores it after deleting an argument; monochrome mode brackets untyped completion suffixes and omits them when the bracketed hint cannot fit. Moving the caret inside a draft now hides the preview and Tab status until the caret returns to the end, matching Tab's actual insertion rule. The linear mode announces that arguments are literal and Tab completion is inline-only. A Linux product-process PTY proves Tab selection, no placeholder submission, strict empty-Run replay, and exact terminal restoration; focused registry/composer/TestBackend tests cover catalog-fed candidates and caret placement. No Provider lookup runs on a keystroke. Native macOS and manual screen-reader review remain open. No commit was made.

### In-progress slice: first-run account setup and protected credentials

Current model-admission sub-slice: connect the existing exact native checker to a first-run selection outside the reviewed model table. Keep the account-visible catalog row and require an explicit effort from the finite wire vocabulary, then a separate billable-check confirmation before creating State or making a Provider call. Use the newly minted account UUID in the check fingerprint, and save the protected account only after the check succeeds. A saved record validates Provider, bounded model ID, explicit effort for an unreviewed tuple, UUID, and key without treating its possibly expired evidence as a permanent record property; every Run still revalidates current exact evidence before Workspace input. The checker remains the sole owner of the three-call/180-second/1,024-output-tokens-per-call bounds. A canceled or failed check may leave an empty private StateRoot or cleared evidence, but no Session, saved key, or runnable selection. Do not activate paid calls in this development environment.

Security pass: the API key, account UUID, billable usage, and future Workspace input are protected. Local explicit confirmation is the only authority for synthetic inference; catalog rows and model IDs are untrusted availability data. Validate the tuple and require consent before State or egress, keep the key out of terminal drawing and errors, reject a failed or expired check at Run admission, and never carry environment-key evidence into the saved-account source. Cancellation cannot retract already accepted remote work, so do not claim it prevents billing. No new endpoint, dependency, remote cap, retry, or Engine seam enters this slice.

Verification map: extend the existing setup and credential tests for reviewed versus unreviewed selections, explicit-effort record validation, and no-check cancellation; use the existing native evidence owner for exact-key/source/UUID gating rather than duplicating its probes. Inspect narrow and linear consent wording and preserve model-picker position on a declined check. Run formatting, strict Clippy, default offline tests, package verification, changed-file review, and auto-learning. A full save/reuse PTY requires an isolated OS credential service; paid live conformance and native macOS remain separate release gates. No commit.

Current sub-slice: serialize OS-store account replacement with one persistent, empty, private StateRoot lock. Compare an unbounded wait with a nonblocking busy result: the latter keeps setup responsive and matches Session lock failure semantics. The lock and the OS-store write must live in the same blocking task so cancellation of the awaiting UI cannot release coordination while the write continues. Recheck the admitted StateRoot against an opened Workspace identity before replacement. No token, account record, or catalog result belongs in the lock file. This prepares the same coordination primitive for future refresh-token rotation without enabling subscription access.

Security pass: the protected assets are the API key, future refresh tokens, and account identity; local setup is the authority. A second process or a malicious StateRoot file may race account replacement, so lock acquisition fails closed on contention and rejects symlink, hard-link, nonempty, public, wrong-owner, and non-regular objects. One fixed lock file adds bounded StateRoot overhead. This does not assert OS-store atomicity, secure memory erasure, or native macOS behavior.

Verification map: extend the Store lock owner with contention, reacquisition, private-file, and alias-overlap cases; retain the isolated native OS-store roundtrip as an ignored platform gate. Run formatting, strict Clippy, the default offline suite, package verification, changed-file review, and auto-learning. No paid call or commit.

Progress: the fixed private StateRoot lock is wired to API-account replacement, held through the blocking OS-store write, and rejects busy and unsafe files. Focused StateRoot tests and a supervised Linux second-process test prove same-StateRoot contention and reacquisition after holder death. `--state-dir` does not change this account root; different process environments can select different default roots through `XDG_STATE_HOME` or `HOME` while still addressing the same fixed OS-store slot. The user chose current-user account access, but whether those alternate roots share one logical account or receive separate OS-store slots is a pending scope decision. The ordinary default-feature suite now passes after the pinned Crossterm repair. Native Keychain/Secret Service and macOS behavior are still untested; no paid call or commit was made.

Slot-scope decision: the [cross-root account finding](../../docs/security/findings/default-account-slot-cross-root.md) records the source-supported release gap. Deriving the keyring slot from the admitted default account root would align each marker and lock with its own credential but requires explicit migration of the existing fixed slot. Keeping one global slot preserves that account identity, but also requires one marker and lock independent of environment-selected roots; adding only a second lock would leave divergent markers. Do not use a hardcoded shared `/tmp` file as a shortcut: [Apple's filesystem guidance](https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/documentation/MacOSX/Conceptual/BPFileSystem/Articles/WhereToPutFiles.html) advises against that path, and its [secure-file guidance](https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/documentation/Security/Conceptual/SecureCodingGuide/Articles/RaceConditions.html) warns about shared temporary-directory races. Neither option may silently overwrite or abandon an existing account. Verify separate-process root selection, slot identity, backend pinning, account replacement, crash recovery, and native Linux/macOS behavior before claiming cross-root safety.

On a bare interactive start without a usable saved account/provider selection, Arany opens the ordinary composer in an unconfigured Session. The empty welcome and status show `/setup`; submitting an objective before setup gets local guidance and starts no Run. Here "access method" means API key versus an eligible subscription-backed account, not `single`/`auto`/`team` collaboration policy. A completed user-level choice seeds new Session defaults; it never resumes a Session implicitly. Explicit `--continue`, `--resume`, `--fork`, and noninteractive `exec` retain their contracts; noninteractive paths fail with actionable setup guidance instead of prompting. Idle `/setup` can replace an account in the current Session, while cancelling it preserves that Session; cancelling explicit pre-Session `--setup` creates no Session or Run.

Compare environment-only key references with protected credential storage: environment references preserve the existing small secret boundary and work on headless hosts, but do not meet the requested saved-key UX. The user now chose an automatic preference for the OS keyring and a private `0600` file under a `0700` user-owned directory only when the keyring is unavailable, with an explicit warning. Pin the chosen backend for each saved account so temporary lock/unavailability never silently switches to a stale or weaker record; replacement must keep one user-level identity and lock across Session StateRoots. File permissions restrict ordinary other-user access, not same-UID, administrator, backup, or physical-device access, and are not encryption. Session defaults continue to persist only the account UUID; replacement changes it so older Sessions fail closed. Headless explicit environment-key paths remain separate, and Arany never imports another CLI's tokens. Subscription refresh tokens remain unwired until their stronger lifecycle and same-user exposure decision is resolved.

Current credential slice: account replacement uses the default user StateRoot independently of a Session's `--state-dir`, with a nonblocking lock held through each backend write. A versioned bounded record pins either a keyring marker without a secret or a file-backed account; the latter is atomically replaced under private-mode, no-follow, single-link, current-owner checks only after a user warning. A locked or pinned keyring never silently falls back. Blocking keyring I/O now runs in a same-binary helper with a five-second deadline and bounded private pipes; timeout kills and reaps it, but an already accepted OS-store write can still finish and is reported as outcome-uncertain. No key enters arguments, environment, SQLite Events, terminal frames, errors, traces, or logs. Different environment-derived default roots and other same-UID processes remain outside the lock's guarantee. Keep ChatGPT-plan token persistence and Run admission disabled.

Security pass: the assets are the native API key, account UUID, future refresh token, and backend choice. The local user authorizes setup; `--state-dir`, Workspace content, a stalled D-Bus peer, a pre-existing file/link, and another same-user process are distinct inputs. Reject unsafe paths and records, cap every read/write and operation duration, hold one user-level lock through replacement, and fail closed on ambiguous backend state. The helper is a privilege boundary only for bounded lifetime, not an app-exclusive same-user secret boundary. The file fallback does not claim encryption or 100% token secrecy.

Verification map: use a private per-user test root and random synthetic account slot, never the live default slot. Focused file-mode, no-follow, hard-link, size, atomic-replacement, malformed-marker, and cross-StateRoot contention checks own file safety. The synthetic stalled-read product test is green; an ignored Linux write-supervisor gate now proves the synthetic record reaches a stalled child, the five-second timeout reports an explicitly uncertain outcome, and its private socket closes after termination. This does not prove an OS-store rollback or exercise a native stalled service. Preserve native isolated keyring and terminal setup cancellation owners. Run formatting, strict Clippy, full offline tests, ordinary package and extracted source archive checks, guarded release build, selected dependency/security review, and documentation auto-learning. Native macOS Keychain/file behavior and real Provider calls remain separate release gates. No commit during this goal.

Official OpenAI documentation describes an open-source Sign in with ChatGPT OAuth path for eligible Plus/Pro account usage. Its current Responses route requires streaming and rejects `max_output_tokens`, so it cannot meet Arany's existing remote per-call output cap. The user chose an explicit exception: offer this route only after a clear warning and affirmative account-level acceptance that local time/read limits do not guarantee a remote token or plan-usage cap. Pin that weaker guarantee and access method in Run admission and receipts; never silently switch to API-key billing or call the route before consent and conformance. Anthropic consumer-subscription access remains unadmitted. Do not ask users to paste subscription cookies, borrow Codex/other-CLI credentials, or send a ChatGPT password to Arany. Sources: [OpenAI quickstart](https://developers.openai.com/siwc/quickstart), [open-source sign-in](https://developers.openai.com/siwc/token-sharing-open-source/sign-in), [preview limitations](https://developers.openai.com/siwc/token-sharing-open-source/preview-limitations), [errors and recovery](https://developers.openai.com/siwc/token-sharing-open-source/errors-and-recovery).

For that route, the selected account's catalog is `GET /v1/models` with visible `models[]` entries (`visibility: "list"`), display names, and model slugs; this differs from the API-key catalog response. Inference must use `store: false`, `stream: true`, account-authorized models, and a bounded stream; only `response.completed` is success. An explicit error, `response.failed`, `response.incomplete`, interrupted stream, or local cap is not success. Consent belongs to the verified ChatGPT account registration and current warning version, not to a CLI install or an API key. Reauthorization, account switch, or warning change must not inherit another account's acceptance. Sources: [models and inference](https://developers.openai.com/siwc/token-sharing-open-source/models-and-inference), [accounts and sessions](https://developers.openai.com/siwc/token-sharing-open-source/profiles-and-sessions), [preview limitations](https://developers.openai.com/siwc/token-sharing-open-source/preview-limitations).

Security pass: protect API keys, OAuth tokens, account identity, Workspace data, and aggregate Run cost. Local human input is setup authority; repository text and Provider output have none. The selected access method and account must be explicit and pinned before catalog/inference; future subscription receipts must distinguish their weaker bound without exposing secrets. Mask key entry, keep it out of shell arguments, Events, logs, telemetry, errors, and terminal scrollback; bind retrieval to the selected Provider/account, never silently switch billing routes, and fail closed on credential-store or consent errors. OAuth requires its own reviewed PKCE/state/nonce/callback, scope, refresh, revocation, storage, no-listener-by-default, and supported-route gates before activation. Bare new attached mode may read the selected OS-store account before a Session exists; no OAuth endpoint is contacted merely by opening a Workspace.

Verification map: state/CLI tests own first-run detection, cancelled/retried setup, saved-default reuse, explicit entry-mode bypass, and noninteractive failure before Workspace input; terminal PTYs own secret masking, EOF/signals/restoration, linear accessibility, and no accidental Session creation. Credential fixtures own OS-store unavailable/locked/wrong-account behavior and redacted diagnostics without real secrets. Existing catalog and Provider tests own model/effort admission after setup. The subscription adapter needs official-route offline wire fixtures, local OAuth threat tests, and explicit opt-in live conformance without Workspace data before paid use; proof must distinguish local bounds from the unavailable remote cap. Update architecture, CLI/Provider/security/terminal contracts, help, and user setup docs when implemented; run formatting, strict Clippy, offline tests, package verification, diff review, and auto-learning. Native macOS, live Provider, and native credential-store checks remain release gates. No commit during this goal.

Current Run-provenance slice: the selected native Provider reports a validated saved API-account UUID only when that key was loaded from the OS store. Engine pins the optional UUID in `RunStarted`; legacy, explicit-environment, custom, and scripted paths remain unbound. Validate native profile and UUIDv7 on replay, and prove the saved path through Provider construction, persisted Event, and reopened RunView without recording a key. The Provider interface is the existing Engine seam; a CLI-supplied RunRequest field would allow the claimed account to drift from the selected adapter. This slice does not pre-admit ChatGPT-plan access or relax the remote output cap. Review source/receipt changes, security boundaries, documentation, formatting, strict lint, offline tests, and package verification before closing it.

Progress: native OpenAI and Anthropic adapters and account-scoped catalogs accept a selected API key directly, with shared 512-byte validation. `exec` and explicit attached selections use only the named environment key; a setup-pinned attached Session uses its saved API account. Bare new attached mode loads that protected account or opens an unconfigured composer with `/setup` guidance; explicit `--setup` opens the wizard before Session creation. Setup lists the full account-visible catalog, offers reviewed model/effort choices, and admits an unreviewed row only after explicit effort, separate billable consent, and a successful exact check bound to the new account UUID. Declining or failing before the OS-store write saves no key; a failure after a successful write may leave a saved account without a Session because the two stores are not transactional. Session defaults pin only the account UUID and expired evidence never becomes a permanent record property. Linux Secret Service and macOS Keychain use keyring 4.2.0 with only `v1`; unsafe Linux D-Bus transports are rejected before keyring access. The OS-store slot is global despite a per-StateRoot replacement lock; full isolated setup/save/reuse PTYs, locked-store behavior, macOS native review, paid live checks, subscription OAuth, and account management beyond one default remain open. No commit was made.

Saved-file reuse evidence (2026-10-01): a Linux screen-reader product process starts bare from a preseeded, private synthetic file account with an intentionally rejected D-Bus transport. It reaches the composer without reopening setup, exits through `/quit`, restores exact terminal settings, and strictly replays one empty Session whose defaults pin only the saved account UUID, Provider, and model. The synthetic key is absent from captured output. Setup PTYs now reuse the existing process guard that verifies both the exact test executable and private State argument before cleanup. Formatting, strict Clippy, the full offline suite, package listing, and an extracted offline source-archive build pass; the archive contains the exact current setup test source. This proves the saved-file read/start path, not the setup write, live Provider compatibility, native keyring behavior, or same-user secrecy. The existing credential-module test separately proves a bounded private-file save/read and backend pinning. A later private-namespace HTTPS fixture covers the private-file setup write/reuse without adding a test-only Provider route; native keyring setup and live catalog access remain separate. No real credential or paid call was used, and no commit was made.

Saved-account replacement evidence (2026-10-01): an explicitly activated Linux product-process gate pins a synthetic file-account UUID in a committed Session, replaces that account, and resumes the older Session with an objective inside a networkless Bubblewrap namespace. The shipped screen-reader executable emits exactly `error: invalid saved account`, exits unsuccessfully, and leaves the journal at its two preexisting Events with no Run; neither the key nor terminal escapes enter output. The private root provides a valid `HOME` and `XDG_STATE_HOME` while rejecting D-Bus, so the gate exercises account identity rather than a missing-home or native-keyring failure. Formatting, strict Clippy, the full offline suite, package listing, and a freshly extracted offline source-archive check pass. It does not prove a real Provider call, full setup/save/reuse, or same-user token secrecy. No real credential, paid call, or commit was involved.

Native helper gate recheck (2026-10-01): the ignored shipped-binary synthetic-slot roundtrip now uses the existing parent deadline and kill/reap guard for both helper processes, including the write-pipe stage, and requires explicit deletion of its unique test credential on success. It passed against this host's unlocked Secret Service; the separate networkless stale-account gate also passed. A stuck OS wallet can no longer strand the helper wait in this test. The roundtrip still does not exercise the shipped setup's fixed default slot, prove locked-store recovery or same-user secrecy, or establish macOS behavior. No real key, paid call, or commit was involved.

Narrow-setup result: the 16-column inline viewport reserves eight rows and no longer cuts compiled choices at three widths. The shorter access-method copy still identifies the unavailable ChatGPT-plan path. Its compact input hint now keeps the byte count, Enter, and Escape visible even at 512 bytes; a rejected choice begins with `Invalid choice` instead of losing its meaning to truncation, while linear mode retains the Ctrl+C cancellation cue. Pure monochrome frames at 16×8, 40×7, and 50×7 prove that the unavailable label, all efforts, separate billable-check choice, and narrow error/action cues remain visible. A supervised 16×8 no-color Linux PTY rejects and corrects an invalid choice, reaches hidden key entry, and exits with Escape before Provider or credential-store access; it finds no key in output or State on disk and verifies exact terminal-setting restoration without alternate-screen use. Native scrollback and the under-eight-row linear fallback are unchanged. Formatting, strict Clippy, the complete offline suite with the temporary Crossterm backend feature, and offline package verification pass. This does not prove full saved-account setup, native macOS, or a fix for the default-feature Crossterm PTY-loss regression. No commit was made.

Current correction: the existing 16-column, no-color setup PTY exposed that Backspace after a rejected character removed a valid choice or key byte instead. Setup now counts a bounded rejected suffix and consumes it before accepted input; later characters cannot silently enter a choice or API key while that suffix remains. The test failed on the old behavior and now passes with two rejected choice characters and a rejected hidden-key suffix; it confirms that a later valid key byte stays blocked, then can be inserted after correction, before canceling without Provider or credential-store access. Formatting, strict Clippy, the full offline suite, and offline package verification pass. This is an input-recovery fix, not a saved-account or native-macOS proof. No commit.

Security pass: setup's locally compiled choices are authority; the terminal width and typed bytes are presentation input only. Do not query a Provider or OS credential store during a test of cancellation. Keep API-key bytes out of frames, process arguments, State, and diagnostics, retain the 512-byte limit, and restore terminal echo/modes before exit. Verification map: extend the existing monochrome setup-frame corpus at 16×8 for every effort, billable consent, and the unavailable subscription label; add one supervised no-color Linux setup PTY that reaches hidden input, cancels before catalog access, compares exact terminal settings, and proves no Session or State. Reuse the existing linear suspend/signal owner. Finish with formatting, strict Clippy, the complete offline suite with the temporary terminal backend, offline package verification, changed-source review, and auto-learning. Native macOS, real OS-store save/reuse, and paid conformance remain separate gates. No commit.

The keyring-backed setup/save/reuse PTY cannot safely run against the current fixed OS-store default slot in this development session: it could replace a user's saved account. The existing native-store test uses a separate random slot, but the shipped setup does not. That ignored Linux test passed on 2026-10-01 against this host's unlocked Secret Service: it saved and read a synthetic record, rejected a different account UUID, deleted the item, and confirmed absence. This proves one native client roundtrip, not backend encryption, other-client exclusion, locked-store recovery, or shipped setup. First isolate a native credential service or account for the keyring product-process gate; an isolated State path is insufficient. This host has no activatable `org.freedesktop.secrets` service in a fresh `dbus-run-session`. Manually starting its installed KDE daemon inside a no-network `bwrap` namespace with a private in-memory home and D-Bus bus exposed the API, but a fresh default collection required a GUI prompt; the synthetic save failed closed as `Unavailable` under offscreen Qt. A test-owned unlockable Secret Service backend is still required for this keyring gate; KDE specifically is not required. The private-file setup path now uses a reviewed namespace-local HTTPS catalog fixture with ordinary TLS validation; it does not establish live catalog availability or authorize use of the live fixed credential slot.

Historical Linux test attempt (2026-10-01): a private KDE Secret Service inside a no-network namespace exposed the standard API, but its prompt did not map to the test display and a synthetic save could not complete. This is a fixture limitation, not a KDE-specific product requirement or a reason to prefer GNOME Keyring. Use any isolated, safely unlockable Secret Service backend for the remaining native product-process save/reuse and locked-store tests; never touch the live default slot. A passing backend test establishes only that backend's current-user behavior, not Arany-only token secrecy.

The [Linux client-isolation finding](../../docs/security/findings/linux-secret-service-client-isolation.md) adds a separate token-storage gate: the selected Secret Service client uses a Diffie–Hellman transfer session, but the standard permits a backend to expose an unlocked item to other client applications. An ignored native test on this host found that `/usr/bin/secret-tool`, as another same-user client, retrieved the exact synthetic record from a random slot and then deleted it; no real key or default slot was used. The user accepted a current-user keyring-first API-key boundary with an explicit private-file warning, not an Arany-only claim. Subscription-token persistence remains disabled until a stronger design and consent are proven. Full isolated-bus setup/save/reuse and locked-item gates remain open.

The [stalled credential-store finding](../../docs/security/findings/stalled-credential-store.md) has a passing product-process synthetic D-Bus read stall: the same-binary helper times out after five seconds, is reaped, closes the connection, and creates no Session or State. An explicit ignored Linux test now sends a synthetic account through the write pipe to a test-built stalled child; timeout reports an uncertain write outcome and the child connection closes. This does not exercise an OS-store write or prove rollback. Native isolated save/read/locked-store and stalled-write behavior, plus macOS Keychain, remain beta gates. The helper receives keys only through bounded pipes, not argv or environment; the account lock stays held through reaping. No real account was used in either stall test.

The [pinned macOS credential source review](../../docs/security/beta-macos-credential-source-review.md) now identifies the selected `keyring/v1` User Keychain implementation and its Security Framework call path. It confirms the intended unsigned-CLI backend selection without proving native save/reuse, locked-store recovery, item access prompts, ACLs, or atomic replacement. Both architecture-specific macOS release journeys still require an isolated native runner. No product code or dependency changed for this review.

Run-provenance result: native Providers now report the validated saved API-account UUID to the Engine, which records it in `RunStarted`; API-key environment, custom, default scripted, and legacy Runs have no such ID. A scripted Engine journey proves the ID survives durable replay, and native adapter/replay validation tests reject malformed identity. Formatting, strict Clippy, the offline suite, and package verification pass. Access-method and weaker-guarantee receipts for the future ChatGPT-plan route remain open.

### First-run setup UX correction

User feedback on 2026-10-02 supersedes the forced initial form. A bare interactive start without an account should show the ordinary chat composer. Typing ordinary text must show that text and its caret; submitting it before setup should give actionable setup-required guidance without starting a Provider Run or disclosing Workspace content. `/setup` should open visible, keyboard-selectable choices for access method, Provider, model, and effort, rather than requiring numeric `1`/`2` entry. Non-secret choices must never be represented only by a byte count; only credential entry remains masked. Preserve draft and caret when closing a selector, keep the no-color and linear/screen-reader paths usable, and retain the existing credential, cost-consent, model-evidence, and State safety gates.

Implementation decision: keep one `AttachedTerminal` owner and one Session path. Bare new entry may inspect the saved account without drawing a setup form; if none exists, create a Session with empty defaults and show its normal composer. A pre-setup objective is rejected locally with `/setup` guidance before Provider admission or Workspace input. Explicit `--setup` and the new idle `/setup` command invoke the same wizard; a successful in-Session wizard persists its selected defaults after the protected account save, while Escape leaves the Session and draft state intact. Inline choices use a highlighted row with Up/Down, Enter, and Escape; linear choices accept visible choice names, with echo disabled only during the key field. The model browser remains the existing catalog selector. This changes the startup and slash-command contract, not the Engine seam or credential backend.

Verification map: the existing setup-frame owner first fails on a non-secret choice shown as a byte count; update it for selected rows at 16/40/80 columns with no color and a separate masked-key assertion. Extend the slash-registry contract and existing attached process scenarios to prove no-account composer input, local setup-required rejection with no Run/Workspace disclosure, `/setup` selection and cancellation, and restored terminal state. Reuse the existing linear screen-reader fixture for visible choices and hidden key. Full account save/reuse must wait for an isolated OS credential service; do not touch the live default slot. Update CLI, terminal, and architecture documentation for the new contract, review that only the key crosses the secret-input path, then run format, strict Clippy, offline tests, package verification, diff review, and auto-learning. Native macOS and live paid Provider gates remain pending.

Current result: the renderer's prior `1B · Enter · Esc` choice frame failed the new regression assertion. Bare no-account startup now creates an unconfigured Session and shows the ordinary composer; an objective is cleared with a leading `/setup` action notice before Run or Workspace input. `/setup` opens the existing wizard in that Session, with focused inline choice rows and named linear choices; only API-key entry disables canonical echo and displays a byte count. The model browser, billable-check consent, and account-save gates remain unchanged. A private-bus, no-color Linux PTY at 16 and 40 columns proves visible per-key inline drawing, local rejection, Up/Down choice focus, Escape closure, terminal restoration, and strict replay with one Session Event and no Run. A screen-reader PTY proves named choices, echo enabled before the key, hidden key entry, suspend/resume, and cancellation. The private-file path now has a separate full offline save/reuse gate; keyring-backed setup still needs an isolated writable service, and live catalog access remains unverified. Native macOS and manual assistive-technology review remain pending.

### In-progress final slice: attached terminal visual and interaction polish

Keep the remaining work after functional and release-critical beta work. The attached UI now owns the full primary screen, with committed transcript above conditional activity, a borderless `Ask Arany` composer with a `> ` lead showing up to four explicit logical lines, and one bottom status row. An empty idle Session has a compact width-aware welcome in the transcript; committed history or agent activity removes it. The composer retains a draft during a Run, has visual-only slash placeholders, bounded agent rows, pickers, and an unchanged linear/screen-reader mode. A notice replaces the status row; unknown idle slash commands retain their bounded draft and caret for correction, while canonical screen-reader input clears the submitted line. The transcript shows confirmed `You:` and `Arany:` messages; the existing `Answer:` result and receipt emit only after terminal restoration. The persistent row shows recorded context-input used/budget bytes only for the active Run at widths of at least 80; active `/status` discloses them at narrower widths. Neither claims a model token-window percentage. Semantic accents can be disabled with `--no-color` or `NO_COLOR`.

Current-tree Linux recheck (2026-10-02): an isolated 80×24 tmux capture of the debug executable shows ordinary `hello` draft text after the `> ` lead, not a byte count; the byte count is reserved for hidden API-key entry. The existing no-account 16/40-column PTY passes visible draft input, local `/setup` guidance, named selector focus, terminal restoration, and Run-free replay. The idle tmux gate passes with both debug and optimized binaries across color, `--no-color`, `NO_COLOR`, and 16–80 columns. This verifies those interactions, not whether the styling meets the user's visual preference or manual assistive-technology usability. Crossterm PR #1116 was still open at this recheck, so the temporary patch remains. No real credential, paid call, or commit was involved.

Current normal layout at the live tail:

```text
  You       committed objective                         navigable transcript
  Arany     committed answer                            navigable transcript
  activity  primary working · child 1 waiting           0–4 rows, only if needed
  Ask Arany label, then > editable draft (up to four lines)
            one blank row
  working · model · context estimate                     persistent bottom row
```

Put the conditional activity shelf **above** the composer and keep status **below** it, separated by one blank row. Anchor the status at the live viewport bottom while the viewport grows upward for agents or draft lines; give transcript the remaining height. Retain at most three stable agent rows plus `+N more`, with `/agents` for details. Use restrained semantic accents for user/assistant labels, focus, attention, and errors, while words, selection markers, and placeholders carry the same meaning under `--no-color` and `NO_COLOR`; pass the admitted color preference to the renderer before introducing a palette. Prefer whitespace and the labeled input lead over extra boxes. At 80+ columns show state, selected model, and a labeled context-input byte estimate when known; at 50–79 retain state and model; below 50 retain state and a short model. Active `/status` discloses the recorded bytes when they do not fit in the persistent row. Do not call byte usage a model context-window percentage or reuse a prior Run's estimate as the current draft's usage.

State contract: **idle** has no empty activity shelf and a clear composer; **typing** keeps real draft/caret distinct from ghost hints and expands upward for multiple lines; **working** leaves the draft editable and shows only committed agent state, with Enter retaining an objective for later explicit submission; **selector open** temporarily owns the screen with a focused row and activation hint, then Escape or selection restores the draft, caret, and history position. A slash command consumed to open a selector is not itself restored as a draft. **Error** uses a textual next step adjacent to the composer and keeps valid input. **Reading older history** retains the same source-text anchor through new committed turns and resize, shows a new-content cue, and returns to the live tail with `Ctrl+L`. The committed objective and answer are sanitized from `SessionView`; raw Provider streams and speculative agent progress never enter the transcript. Inline `Ctrl+O` inserts an explicit newline while Enter keeps submission; linear mode retains its Ctrl+D continuation. Terminal-theme contrast and manual assistive-technology review remain separate evidence.

The user approved replacing native-scrollback navigation because it could not keep the input visible while reading older content. The primary-screen viewport does not use alternate screen; Arany now owns historical navigation and literal find, while `show` remains the complete export. Native terminal selection still works on visible cells, but emulator search of old physical output is not a complete Session search. Additional native crash/recovery and cross-platform evidence is required before declaring parity.

First visual result: the bounded activity rows now render above the composer, while the status or notice stays directly below it; idle has no empty shelf. One Linux tmux owner runs the shipped `--no-color` attached command with a synthetic environment key, captures the live pane at 80, 50, and 40 columns, and checks the composer/status order, terminal restoration, and empty-Session replay. A second, loopback-only tmux owner captures an active Run at 40 columns, checks activity above the editable draft and notice below it, then proves the draft remains after the Run and was not submitted. TestBackend covers 40/50/79/80/120-cell activity, draft, error, placeholder, and monochrome rows. This is live-tail placement evidence, not proof of fixed input while browsing historical scrollback, a semantic color palette, or native macOS/assistive-technology behavior. Formatting, strict Clippy, full offline tests, and package verification pass; no commit was made.
