Setup polish result (2026-10-01): the first-run inline step occupies a centered seven-row region, or eight rows at 16–19 columns, without a surrounding box. Its content is now also horizontally centered in a maximum 64-column measure; narrower layouts retain their full available width. It separates brand, step title, bounded instruction, byte-count/action hints, and recoverable notice; it never receives key content. TestBackend covers 16×8, 40×12, 80×24, and 120×24 centered layouts, 16/40/50-column effort, consent, and error copy, monochrome cells, and no borders. Isolated native tmux captures confirm the first-run frame at 80×24, 40×12, and 16×8 without color; Escape exits without creating a Session. The native 16×8 no-color setup PTY separately proves hidden key entry, rejected-byte correction, cancellation before State creation, and exact terminal restoration. The linear/screen-reader setup is unchanged. This does not claim a complete native keyring save/reuse journey, macOS behavior, or manual assistive-technology review; no commit was made.

Help discoverability slice (2026-10-01): a real 80-column startup showed that `/help` was a one-row notice cut off before the command list. Inline idle and active `/help` now open a local scrollable panel sourced from the closed slash registry, with a selected command description and Enter/Escape return; the linear/screen-reader notice remains append-only. The panel owns no canonical state, credential, Provider, catalog, or Workspace read and preserves the existing composer and history anchor. A TestBackend owner checks first/last registered commands and monochrome layout at 16×8, 40×12, and 80×24. The isolated Linux tmux gate navigates from `/help` to `/exit` at 16 columns, closes to the same idle composer, and separately opens and closes help while a synthetic Run is held; the screen-reader PTY still sees the full help notice. A supervised inline PTY opens help over a held synthetic Run, then Ctrl+C closes the panel, cancels the Run, restores the terminal, and leaves one strictly replayable cancelled Run without an answer. A separate 16×8 monochrome product PTY now confirms that SIGTERM with idle help open restores exact terminal settings, shows no credential, and adds no Run or Event beyond Session setup. The raw stream can split a visible header with cursor controls, so that test waits for the complete footer before signaling. This gate also exposed that raw-mode Enter must be carriage return, unlike a canonical-line boundary; the testing rule already states that distinction. Formatting, strict Clippy, the full offline suite, offline package listing, and a fresh extracted source-archive check passed for the initial slice; formatting, strict Clippy, and the full offline suite pass after the new signal test. Native macOS and manual assistive-technology review remain open; no commit.

Completion-hint polish (2026-10-01): a real 40×12 monochrome frame showed `/provider openai` still advertising Tab after Tab had already completed the choice. The terminal-local preview now names the unique Tab result for a partial Provider, model, or effort value, then says Enter submits once the known value is complete. It does not change parser admission or claim that an active Run accepts a locked command. The existing closed-registry test owns the hint transition; the real tmux frame showed `/provider o[penai]` with `Tab completes openai` and then `/provider openai` with `Enter submits this command`. Formatting, strict Clippy, and the full offline suite pass. The native 16–80-column color/monochrome gate passed twice with `rtk proxy cargo test`; the `rtk cargo test` shortcut was separately confirmed to deny loopback binds and fail private-State admission in this environment, not to expose an Arany regression. The hint uses only admitted local choices, adds no State, credential, catalog, or Provider I/O on a keystroke, and leaves linear/screen-reader output unchanged. Native macOS and manual assistive-technology review remain open; no commit.

Security pass: the welcome, input lead, and setup labels are compiled copy, not Workspace or Provider content. Setup passes only a bounded secret byte count into drawing, never the key; its hidden-input and terminal-restoration owner still passes. Existing bounded, sanitized history, draft, status, and slash-preview paths still own all other untrusted text; the change adds no network, credential, persistence, terminal-mode, or Provider authority. The existing hostile-text and cursor tests remain in the passing suite. Visual evidence does not establish native macOS terminal restoration or assistive-technology usability.

Pre-migration UI review (2026-10-01): the earlier native-scrollback interface already placed conditional activity above a bounded multiline composer and one bottom status row, but could not keep input visible while reading older content. Linux tmux gates passed its live-tail color/monochrome, retained-draft, private-State, and restoration behavior. This evidence informed the approved migration below; it was not proof of historical-reading continuity.

Approved fixed-bottom migration (implemented on Linux; verification ongoing): the earlier inline viewport kept the live-tail composer visible but terminal-native historical scrolling moved it off screen. The selected full-height primary-screen Ratatui viewport does not enter alternate screen. It keeps transcript, conditional activity, bounded multiline composer, and status in order. Retaining inline mode would preserve emulator search and selection but could not satisfy fixed input and stable historical reading. Arany now owns `PageUp`/`PageDown`, literal `Ctrl+F` find, and `Ctrl+L` live-tail return; `arany show` remains the complete deterministic export. Native terminal selection remains usable on visible cells, but emulator search of old physical output is no longer a complete Session search. Native macOS, manual accessibility, and additional history fault/recovery gates remain open.

Narrow completion follow-up (2026-10-01): the old `Tab completes openai` label hid the choice at 16 columns. The terminal-local status now uses `Tab: openai` or `Tab: /help`, leaving each complete result visible in a real 16×8 monochrome tmux frame. The draft, ghost, parser, and no-keystroke-I/O boundaries are unchanged.

At 40 columns, the proposed states occupy the same screen regions:

```text
idle       | chat: latest committed turns | no activity | Ask Arany: empty       | ready · model
typing     | chat: same reading position  | no activity | Ask Arany: draft/caret | ready · model
working    | chat: committed turns only   | agent rows  | Ask Arany: draft/caret | working · model
selector   | temporary focused list       | hidden      | retained off-screen    | Enter/Esc guidance
error      | chat: same reading position  | as before   | Ask Arany: valid draft | Error: next action
reading    | chat: anchored older turns   | agent rows  | Ask Arany: draft/caret | new text · Ctrl+L
```

The terminal owns the history cursor, focused panel, dimensions, and render cache; none enters Session Events or the Engine. Derive transcript content only from the already-loaded `SessionView`, never by a file or Provider read during drawing. Sanitize before measuring cells and render only the visible window; do not duplicate the full Session transcript in a second unbounded buffer. The Store's existing 256 MiB database and 10,000-Event Session replay bounds are not a license for a full-width render allocation. At the live tail, new committed turns advance the transcript; while reading older content, anchor by a stable Run/message/line identity and show a textual new-content cue until `Ctrl+L` returns to the live tail. `PageUp`/`PageDown` navigate without editing the composer, and `Ctrl+F` opens bounded transcript find. Preserve existing Up/Down and Home/End composer meanings; typing remains available while reading older content. Selectors restore the same draft, grapheme caret, and history anchor; changing Sessions starts at the selected Session's tail only after the existing empty-draft rule. Resize remaps the anchor rather than jumping to the end. Keep the screen-reader/linear path append-only, without raw mode or redraw.

Implementation order: (1) add one terminal-owned lazy visible-window projection and TestBackend frames for the states above at 16/20/40/80 columns, Unicode and `NO_COLOR`; (2) attach explicit history navigation and find, with bounded work per input and deterministic not-found/older-history feedback; (3) switch the normal attached viewport to full-height primary screen, then adapt committed `You:`/`Answer:` presentation, selectors, suspend/reacquire, and receipt output without changing `exec`/`show` bytes; (4) run native Linux PTY/tmux journeys for active updates while scrolled back, resize, search, selection, picker return, SIGINT/SIGTSTP/SIGTERM, panic/fault restoration, and exact State replay. Keep native macOS, terminal-theme contrast, and Orca/VoiceOver as explicit beta gates on the later machine. A new mode is not considered complete if it loses the current fixed live-tail composer, retained draft, bounded activity, no-color meaning, or accessible linear behavior.

Security pass: untrusted objectives, model IDs, and assistant text remain inert through the existing sanitizer; no raw Provider stream, terminal control, or Workspace text grants UI authority. The immutable committed `SessionView` is the sole transcript source. Find input is capped at 64 UTF-8 bytes, rendered rows at 4,096 cells, and one materialized message is bounded by the existing Event cap; drawing and keystrokes do no credential, catalog, State, or network I/O. New committed turns append to a stable-width index, but initial indexing, width-change rebuild, and explicit find can still scan the bounded 256 MiB Store history synchronously: responsiveness at the largest admitted history remains an open beta performance gate. The screen owner must restore cursor, wrapping, raw and mouse modes on every current exit path. Verification owners are the existing TestBackend frame corpus, Linux PTY/tmux attached journeys, exact linear fixture, and read-only reopened replay; formatting, strict Clippy, the full offline suite, package verification, source/diff review, and auto-learning close each behavior slice. No commits during this goal.

Primary-screen implementation result: `terminal/history.rs` indexes committed objective/answer row counts, draws only the visible rows, preserves an older source-text anchor through resize and newly committed turns, and marks new content without moving the draft. Stable-width snapshots now append a newly committed objective or answer after checking the prior Run identity, instead of reindexing settled turns; a discontinuity still rebuilds. The terminal clears its primary-screen cache on reacquisition so confirmed output cannot remain as a duplicate phantom row. `PageUp`/`PageDown`, `Ctrl+F`, and `Ctrl+L` are terminal-local, with the current 8 KiB composer and linear/screen-reader path unchanged. The Linux tmux gate now proves an eight-row, no-color history search while a synthetic Run is held, a stable older reading position and new-content cue as its answer commits, return to the live tail, retained draft, one in-viewport `You:`/`Arany:` pair, confirmed stdout answer, terminal restoration, and strict replay. It also confirms that opening and closing quick actions from that older reading position restores the exact screen and draft, and that normal suspend/resume preserves all seven reading/composer rows while only the bottom notice changes. The test identifies the private Arany child before resuming it and guards cleanup if it stops unexpectedly. Existing picker mouse PTYs were updated to the new bounded panel geometry. The staged objective/answer unit test, explicit tmux gate, formatting, strict Clippy, full offline suite, and extracted-source check pass. Native macOS, manual Orca/VoiceOver and terminal-theme review, largest-history responsiveness, and history navigation through terminal-loss or picker faults remain open. No commit was made.

Packaged terminal gates (2026-10-02): the existing ignored Linux idle and active tmux journeys now accept an explicit absolute `ARANY_RELEASE_BINARY` while retaining Cargo's binary by default. Both passed against the extracted Linux bundle, including a rerun after first-run guidance was added. The rerun exposed stale help-count assertions: `/setup` made the closed registry 18 commands, and the real 16-column panel correctly showed `Commands 1/18`. The owning tmux expectations and architecture/terminal command lists now match the compiled registry. The idle journey covers color, `--no-color`, and `NO_COLOR` through 16×8, quick actions/help, pane/client TTY restoration, and strict empty-Session replay. The active journey uses the same candidate for data-free custom conformance and a held loopback Run, then checks retained draft, history search/reading, selector and suspend return, confirmed answer, exact TTY restoration, and strict replay. The process cleanup guard requires the selected executable and private State. The bundle was rebuilt from a verified source archive under an allowlisted top-level environment; all 791 extracted member hashes pass and the archive contains the current test source. The earlier manual candidate inspection separately covered long find-query tail display and draft return at 16×8; its executable has the same SHA-256. This does not make the candidate redistributable or prove paid native Provider or macOS behavior. No commit was made.

Large-history measurement (2026-10-01): on this Linux host with load 0.73 and CPU `some avg10=0.00` before the run, one optimized in-memory 94 MiB ASCII corpus took 14.5 ms to index and 13.9 ms to reindex at a new width; the corresponding Unicode corpus took 248.1 and 249.0 ms. Already indexed visible redraws took less than 1 ms in both cases, and a literal miss took 13.5/17.1 ms. A separate optimized file-backed fixture seeded three synthetic Sessions, then reopened and strictly replayed a 1,600-Run/9,601-Event Session with 65,528,000 transcript bytes in a 263,958,528-byte SQLite file: replay took 204.4 ms, indexing 9.6 ms, visible projection 1.1 ms, and width-change reindexing 9.8 ms. Its 248,488 KiB process high-water includes fixture seeding and other Sessions; it is not an incremental product-process RSS result. These single host-specific samples narrow the initial-index/resize cost, but do not define or pass a worst-case Unicode latency budget, measure a complete 256 MiB Unicode Session, or replace the native tmux keyboard proof.

History-index responsiveness sub-slice: avoid materializing every message merely to count its wrapped rows, then count independent Runs across at most four joined, read-only workers only when a rebuild covers at least 4 MiB. Preserve the existing committed `SessionView`, message order, source-byte resize anchor, 4,096-cell cap, and incremental-append path; no State, Provider, credential, or network access belongs to these workers. The owning verification compares counted and materialized rows for ASCII, Unicode, bidi/control escapes, empty lines, and narrow widths; a default 4 MiB rebuild checks order, find, and resize, while an ignored optimized named-host measurement records initial, redraw, resize, and miss-search costs for synthetic approximately 94 MiB ASCII and Unicode histories. Run the Linux tmux history journey, formatting, strict Clippy, full offline tests, extracted-source check, changed-source review, and auto-learning. This is not a native macOS or complete 256 MiB Store-performance claim. No commit during this goal.

On this Linux host with low short-term CPU pressure, the approximately 94 MiB ASCII case fell from 3.20 s initial / 3.23 s resize to 13–15 ms for each after count-only indexing and bounded parallelism. Three final optimized runs put the Unicode/control case at 237–242 ms initial and 238–247 ms resize; stable redraw stayed below 2 ms and a missing literal search below 20 ms. The count/ordering tests pass. A near-cap file-backed State is measured below, and the separate 1,600-Run tmux gate proves visible sequential input during a held Run. Responsiveness during simultaneous committed updates with the largest admitted Unicode/mixed history, native macOS behavior, and a larger quiet-host latency distribution remain open before closing the history-performance gate.

File-backed extension (2026-10-01): an ignored, explicitly activated test writes only synthetic valid Events through the normal Store API into a private temporary State root, closes it, then reopens read-only for strict replay and terminal History indexing. The default profile accepts a bounded 1–1,600 Runs, with six Events per finished Run and at most 9,601 Events in its Session. The opt-in `ARANY_TEST_HISTORY_FILL_STATE` profile adds one Session with the same Run count and a third with 240 Runs; at the maximum it writes 20,643 global Events while each Session stays under the 10,000-Event replay cap. Linux also reports process peak RSS. No Workspace input, credentials, Provider call, network destination, or new production authority is involved. Verification separates seed, open, replay, initial index, visible-row materialization, and resize; this is not a native PTY or macOS test.

One optimized Linux x86_64 sample at 1,600 Runs produced 65,528,000 transcript bytes and a 122,793,984-byte SQLite file. Read-only reopen took 0.29 ms, strict replay 181 ms, initial index 9.7 ms, visible-row materialization 1.6 ms, and resize 9.0 ms. Before the Store append optimization, seeding all 9,601 Events took 715 s because each append resolved the persisted prefix; at 512 Runs / 3,073 Events it took 72 s. That former write cost was separate from terminal index latency. Neither original sample established a quiet-host distribution, concurrent-input responsiveness, a near-cap State, or macOS behavior.

The expanded three-Session profile produced a 263,958,528-byte (251.7 MiB) SQLite file, about 0.27 MiB below the effective 252 MiB write-admission threshold after the Store's 4 MiB headroom. Across three optimized Linux runs at 1,600 Runs in the selected Session, seeding all three Sessions took 2.92–2.97 s, strict read-only replay of the selected Session took 212–216 ms, initial terminal indexing 9.5–10.7 ms, visible-row materialization 1.2–1.5 ms, and resize 9.3–9.9 ms. The process peak RSS was 246,476–248,360 KiB. This establishes one near-limit file-backed Linux workload, not an arbitrary Event-mix, live terminal event-loop responsiveness, native macOS result, or final release latency distribution. The ignored test is synthetic, bounded, and offline; it adds no production state, authority, or egress.

Store append-cost result (2026-10-01): the prior 512-Run profile attributed late-append time to full persisted resolution (32.9 ms) and candidate-prefix reduction (19.8 ms), not SQLite commit (0.063 ms). Ordinary appends now use the reducer's open-prefix transition and may reuse one bounded direct-Session projection owned by the SQLite thread. Reuse requires the same Session, unchanged same-connection SQLite [`data_version`](https://www.sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_data_version), and the next global Event sequence inside an immediate transaction; another connection's commit, a Session switch, failed append, fork, or compaction candidate forces strict replay. Fork lineage is never cached, cumulative serialized Event payload in the projection is capped at 128 MiB, and the 10,000-Event replay ceiling rejects the next append. The cache is installed only after commit. On this Linux host, optimized synthetic seeding of 512 Runs / 3,073 Events took 0.453 s, versus 48.0 s after the earlier reducer change and 71.2 s before it; 1,600 Runs / 9,601 Events took 1.387 s, with 150 ms read-only strict replay and a 122,793,984-byte database. These are named-host samples, not a latency distribution or a full 256 MiB State guarantee; cold paths and projections over the payload cap still scan the persisted journal. The ordinary Session journey, invalidation/failed-insert cases, file-backed replay limit, and Store process-death gate protect this slice. No Provider, Workspace, credential, network, schema, or public interface changed.

Security pass: canonical Events, sensitive Session text, and compaction lineage are the assets; persisted rows and candidate Events are untrusted until strict decoding and transition validation. The admitted StateRoot, sole SQLite owner thread, and immediate write transaction remain the authority and effective boundary. A cold append replays the persisted prefix; a hot append uses only a previously validated projection after checking cross-connection change and global sequence, then validates the candidate transition. Compaction and fork retain direct-prefix and lineage checks. Existing 64 KiB Event, 10,000-Event replay, 256 MiB database, 128 MiB cached-payload, and commit-before-acknowledgement bounds remain. Failure drops the projection without publishing the candidate. This adds no data destination or credential access and does not claim encryption, tamper evidence against direct file changes, or native macOS behavior. Verification maps to the file-backed replay-limit test, invalidation and rollback tests, Session success/error journey, process-death gate, optimized near-limit measurement, formatting, strict Clippy, full offline tests, source archive, and changed-source review.

The explicit Linux tmux history journey first captured the old eight-row frame immediately after resizing its pane to 24 rows, then falsely reported a missing `You:` block. The fixture now waits for the completed 24-row frame, with its finished status on the last row, before asserting the transcript. The corrected gate passed once and then five repeated runs; no terminal or Engine behavior was changed to satisfy that synchronization requirement. The same native gate now suspends and resumes with the Agents quick action focused over an older reading position, then checks exact selector focus, reading-frame return, and retained draft. This narrows picker interruption evidence on Linux without proving terminal-loss recovery, 16-column picker faults, macOS, or assistive-technology behavior.

Historical-resize follow-up (2026-10-01): the existing Linux tmux journey now narrows an eight-row, 40-column older-history frame to 24 columns and returns to 40 while retaining the visible committed objective and multiline draft. It requires the original frame to return byte for byte before continuing through the existing selector, suspend/resume, live-tail, terminal-restoration, and strict-replay assertions. Two focused native activations pass. The first attempted 50-column assertion was invalid because the short displayed content produced identical captures at 40 and 50 columns; the test now uses a width that visibly changes the history status. The pure history owner separately checks source-byte anchoring across reflow. This proves one Linux resize round trip, not arbitrary content/width mixes or native macOS behavior.

Session-picker interruption follow-up (2026-10-01): the existing Linux process owner now exercises its 16×8 picker after the final Escape footer and frame-close marker, then delivers SIGTERM. Mouse capture is disabled before shell restoration, the exact TTY settings return, and read-only strict replay leaves the seeded Session unchanged. The same owner closes its separate stderr PTY after a complete 16×8 picker frame, triggers redraw, and proves raw-mode restoration and unchanged replay. Its process guard now reuses the existing exact-binary-and-private-State validation instead of a weaker duplicate. The first narrow signal test incorrectly expected contiguous footer words across Ratatui cursor controls; the corrected final-frame gate passes. Other narrow picker faults, native macOS, and manual accessibility remain open. No Provider, credential, post-seed State mutation, or commit was involved.

Selector-return gap: source inspection shows `/sessions`, `/models`, and `/agents` consume the submitted command before opening their panels. The composer itself preserves text and caret, but there is no non-destructive way to open a selector over an unrelated draft; the state contract above is therefore only proved for an already-open panel, not for the user's requested draft-to-selector-to-draft journey. The smallest scoped interaction is one inline quick-selector shortcut that opens the existing Session, model, or agent panel without consuming the composer; closing it must restore the same bytes, grapheme-boundary caret, and live-tail viewport. Session switching while a draft exists needs an explicit same-session rule or confirmation so text is not silently carried to another Session. Keep canonical screen-reader command input and native scrollback unchanged. Verify cancel and successful selection through a synthetic native PTY at 40/80 columns, plus a no-color and terminal-restoration case, before claiming this behavior.

First implementation step: `Ctrl+K` opens a fixed, local three-row quick selector for Models, Agents, and Sessions, with Up/Down, Enter, and Escape. The selector performs no catalog or credential I/O until an action is chosen, and it borrows the existing composer without taking or reconstructing it. Models and Agents reuse their current panels; Sessions is visibly unavailable while a draft is nonempty, and otherwise uses the current Session picker. Linear mode keeps its existing slash-command path. A 40-column no-color product-process gate must type a grapheme-aware draft, open and close the quick selector and Agent inspector, then prove the exact caret by insertion, empty-Run replay, and terminal restoration. TestBackend owns selected-row and 40/80-cell layout; the existing key-map corpus owns `Ctrl+K`. No Provider call or new canonical Event belongs to this entry point.

Result: idle inline `Ctrl+K` now draws that five-row selector without mutating the composer or enabling mouse capture. The 40-column no-color product-process journey returns through Escape and the Agent inspector with the same emoji-adjacent caret, rejects a Session switch while the draft exists, exits with exact terminal settings, and reopens one Session with no Run. The existing exact-custom model PTY now opens through the quick Models action and proves selected-default replay without inference. Separate minimal PTYs prove both `/sessions` and the quick Sessions action close normally with an empty draft; each passed eight repeated activations. All four entry paths reuse one selector dispatcher. An initial test sent keys after only the picker header, before its final frame and cursor query had settled, producing an apparent rendering failure and injected cursor-response bytes. Waiting for the completed frame removed that fixture race; no terminal-owner change was warranted. TestBackend covers focused and unavailable rows at 40/80 columns in color and monochrome; the existing key corpus covers Ctrl+K. Formatting, strict Clippy, the full offline suite (198 passed, 30 ignored), and offline package verification pass. The cross-Session draft rule, native macOS, manual assistive-technology and theme review, and historical-scrollback position remain open. No paid call or commit was made.

Monochrome placeholder refinement: slash hints retain their bracketed visual distinction but no longer use reduced intensity under `--no-color` or `NO_COLOR`; color-enabled inline mode keeps the existing subdued hint. The existing TestBackend owner failed before the renderer change and now checks both palettes at 40/80 columns. The 40-column real Linux PTY passes Tab completion, retained correction, replay, and terminal restoration; the two explicit native tmux gates pass idle color/monochrome layouts at 40/50/80 columns and an active retained multiline draft. Formatting, strict Clippy, the full offline suite (193 passed, 30 ignored), and offline package verification pass. This changes no draft, command, status, Provider, or native-scrollback behavior. Native macOS and manual assistive-technology review remain open. No commit.

Narrow model-browser result: below 80 columns, inline rows put a textual `reviewed`, `check`, or `exact` classification before the model ID. Long ASCII IDs show both ends instead of losing distinguishing suffixes; escaped or non-ASCII text retains the terminal sanitizer, and the full ID remains available through `provider models`. Status follows only when the ID fits with room left. This changes no selection, capability evidence, Provider request, key binding, or scrollback contract. The existing TestBackend owner first failed on a 40-column unreviewed row, then passed for reviewed, unreviewed, custom, hostile, long-prefix, and exact-fit rows. Its Linux product-process owner opens the exact custom catalog at 40 columns with `--no-color`, sees `[exact]` before selecting, restores the terminal, and strictly replays the selected default. Formatting, strict Clippy, the full offline suite (including linear catalog behavior), the focused PTY, and package verification pass. Native macOS and manual accessibility remain open. No commit.

Error-editing pass: idle Enter previously consumed the composer before `parse_submission` could reject an unknown slash command, so the inline user lost the typo and caret. Validate the bounded draft before consuming it; an unknown inline command keeps its exact text and cursor for correction, while a valid command or objective retains existing submission semantics. In canonical screen-reader mode, a submitted line cannot be edited afterward; clear its rejected draft with a labeled notice so the next physical line starts fresh. This is terminal-local presentation state, not Session or Provider state. Security pass: an invalid slash command remains a local notice, never a Provider objective or canonical Event; the existing 8 KiB bound and sanitizer remain authoritative. Verification map: extend the existing Linux slash-completion PTY to submit a typo, observe the error, correct it using Backspace and Tab, and verify the chosen Provider plus strict empty-Run replay and TTY restoration. Add a linear PTY case proving the next physical command is not fused with the rejected one. Run formatting, strict Clippy, full offline tests, package verification, changed-source review, and auto-learning. No commit during this goal.

Result: the inline submit path validates before taking the composer text. The existing slash-completion PTY first failed with Anthropic still selected; after the fix, its 40-column `--no-color` case corrects `/provder` with seven Backspaces and Tab, selects OpenAI, preserves an empty-Run Session, and restores the TTY. The new canonical screen-reader case confirms the rejected line is announced and cannot fuse with a later valid `/provider openai`. Both focused cases passed five repeated activations; formatting, strict Clippy, the full offline suite, and offline package verification pass. This preserves only parser-rejected inline text; once a valid objective begins a Run, recovery remains governed by committed Session state rather than automatic resubmission. No commit was made.

Recognized-argument correction: validate pure idle command syntax and already selected Provider/model prerequisites before consuming the composer. A rejected inline command keeps its text and caret; canonical screen-reader input clears the submitted physical line. Operational failures after State access, credential lookup, or an attempted mutation still consume the command, because restoring it could encourage a duplicate effect. Reject unexpected arguments on no-argument controls, especially `/new` and `/quit`, before any action. Keep the validation in the attached CLI boundary, not Engine or the terminal renderer. Security pass: rejected controls create no Session Event, Provider request, or effect, and notices must not echo secrets or untrusted arguments. Verification map: extend the existing 40-column, no-color PTY with invalid `/agents` followed by Backspace correction and strict empty-Run replay; retain the linear rejected-line owner; add focused pure validation cases for unexpected arguments and prerequisites. Update terminal behavior guidance, then run formatting, strict Clippy, offline tests, package verification, source review, and auto-learning. No commit during this goal.

Result: the attached CLI now checks locally invalid arguments before taking the composer. The 40-column, no-color PTY first failed with unchanged `auto` policy after `/agents bad`; it now edits that retained draft to `/agents team 2`, persists the chosen policy, creates no Run, and restores terminal settings. The screen-reader PTY confirms a rejected `/agents bad` line does not fuse with the next physical command. A focused validation test covers unexpected no-argument input (including `/new` and `/quit`), invalid IDs and policy, and missing Provider/model prerequisites. Operational errors remain consumed; this slice does not claim rollback after an attempted effect. Formatting, strict Clippy, the full offline suite, and package verification pass; no paid call, credential-store read, or commit was made.

Active-Run slash correction: the active composer already retains an objective entered during a Run, but a parser-rejected slash command currently clears it. Distinguish a local rejection from a handled read-only/locked command in the existing attached control result; keep the exact bounded draft and caret after an inline rejection, while clearing a submitted canonical screen-reader line so it cannot join the next physical command. Do not alter active command authority, Run scheduling, Provider input, or canonical Events. Security pass: the rejected text remains terminal-local under the 8 KiB composer bound and inert output sanitizer; it cannot steer or bill a Provider call. Verification map: add a focused control regression for the rejected result and extend the existing 40-column no-color active tmux gate to correct a typo while a synthetic Provider is held, then assert the original single Run and terminal restoration. Verify the canonical-line clear path, update terminal/architecture guidance, and run formatting, strict Clippy, offline tests, package verification, source review, and auto-learning. No commit during this goal.

Result: a parser-rejected active slash command now has a distinct local result. Inline mode preserves the draft for Backspace and Tab correction; canonical screen-reader mode clears its submitted line before the next command. The control test failed before the fix. The existing 40-column no-color tmux gate now corrects `/provder` during a held synthetic Provider call, observes the locked `/provider` notice, retains a subsequent multiline draft, and replays one Run. A real screen-reader PTY proves the rejected line cannot join the following `/provider` command. Formatting, strict Clippy, the full offline suite, the explicit tmux gate, and offline package verification pass. No commit.

Security pass: user objectives, model names, Agent summaries, and errors are untrusted display data. ## Ownership and seams

| Responsibility | Owner | Interface and ordering | Seam |
|---|---|---|---|
| CLI grammar and mode selection | `main.rs` | Validated command to Engine; exit after durable result | In-process |
| Run coordination and budgets | `engine.rs` behind `lib.rs` | One committed transition before any observation; bounded cancellation | In-process |
| Resume, fork, compaction, context | `session.rs` and private `session/` files | Typed committed Session facts and bounded context | In-process |
| Provider invocation | `provider.rs` | Semantic `Finish` or `Delegate`; selected profile fixed per Run | Local-substitutable: scripted, OpenAI, Anthropic, verified custom |
| SQLite Events | `store.rs` | Bounded append/replay requests, one owner thread, transaction acknowledgement | In-process ownership isolation |
| Semantic and terminal output | `presentation.rs`, `terminal.rs` | Pure projections; terminal alone owns modes and input | In-process |
| Opt-in trace export | `telemetry.rs` | Bounded, lossy, safe facts after commit | In-process output isolation |

The Engine owns every state transition. No vendor wire type, SQLite handle, path-open capability, or terminal type crosses its public interface. Unknown Event kinds and illegal transitions fail replay.

## Interface and resource contract

Implement the command grammar, Event schema, lifecycle, provider outcomes, and limits in the canonical architecture and decision register. The Event journal is canonical; views and compaction snapshots are derived. Each Run pins provider/model, Workspace snapshot facts, collaboration policy, and one aggregate budget before `RunStarted`. Calls and children consume that budget without retries or fallback. Accepted Events commit before the reducer or presentation sees them. A process crash leaves an interrupted prefix.

The fixed beta limits include an 8 KiB user objective, 64 KiB Event payload, 256 MiB SQLite cap with 4 MiB admission headroom, 64 store requests, at most 32 queued updates, at most eight direct children, 4,096 output tokens per call, and 120/300-second call/Run deadlines. Durable limits reject or backpressure visibly; content is never silently truncated. The attached progress observer uses one latest-snapshot slot within that update ceiling and explicitly coalesces superseded display states, never canonical Events.

## Security model

The protected assets are credentials, Workspace input, Session history, canonical Events, and terminal integrity. CLI selections and trusted user configuration establish authority before any Workspace content is read. The Workspace, provider output, replayed text, and custom endpoint responses are attacker-controlled data. Immutable Run settings are the normalized authorization decision; the effective beta capabilities are bounded read-only Workspace input and Arany-issued requests to one selected Provider. Auxiliary OS certificate-related connections are outside that payload authority. Children receive narrower assignment capsules. There is no sandbox or Tool execution claim.

State is private, owner-checked, no-follow, and outside the admitted Workspace. Inputs are handle-relative, no-follow, bounded snapshots. Only the selected Provider credential is loaded. Native origins are compiled; custom endpoints require exact current data-free conformance. Human output neutralizes controls and bidi; JSONL is serialized. Secrets and raw content do not enter diagnostics or telemetry. An unavailable capability, malformed state, missing evidence, limit breach, or cancellation fails closed with a safe typed result. Every dependency, native/build script, procedural macro, and application `unsafe` is reviewed before release.

## Execution and verification map

### Completed slice: Linux resident-memory gate

Use the shipped release executable in a supervised screen-reader PTY, with a checked loopback custom Provider and exactly two 128 KiB included files. Capture the Arany PID before `exec`, wait for the first idle input prompt, then read that process's Linux `smaps_rollup` RSS baseline. Submit one direct objective and hold the Provider response after it receives and verifies both full includes. Take exact RSS snapshots before the response, after its first half has been sent, and after the Run returns to idle; after process exit, read [zsh's `time` `%M` maximum resident-memory value](https://zsh.sourceforge.io/Doc/Release/Parameters.html) for that child. Compare the greatest observed value minus idle with the researched 32 MiB limit. [Linux documents](https://docs.kernel.org/6.9/filesystems/proc.html) `/proc/status` RSS and high-water fields as approximate, and this host showed the high-water field decreasing between samples; the timed peak has also been slightly below a simultaneous exact snapshot, so retain the maximum rather than assuming the measures are strictly ordered. Strict read-only replay and exact synthetic output are correctness checks. This is a Linux platform measurement, not native macOS evidence.

Security pass: the PTY and loopback server are test-owned and bounded, the product gets a cleared environment with one synthetic key, the State root is private outside the Workspace, and both include files are fixed-size inert bytes. The local request parser caps the body, the reader caps terminal output, and all process, channel, and socket waits have parent deadlines and kill/reap cleanup. The absolute zsh executable is test-owned measurement machinery, not a product Tool. No paid route, remote destination, Tool, or telemetry exporter is involved. `/proc` is read only for the exact supervised product PID.

Verification map: keep the ignored Linux release fixture in its own private performance file, verify the idle and post-Run stages, received include bytes, successful answer, no secret output, and one finished replayed Run. Run the named-host measurement, investigate a limit miss before changing product code, update the physical tree and plan result, then run formatting, strict Clippy, offline tests, package inspection, changed-source review, and auto-learning. OTLP saturation, native macOS, paid Provider, terminal fault/accessibility, and advisory gates remain open. No commits during this goal.

### Completed Linux gate: startup bound with two 32 KiB included files

Measure 10 warmup and 100 release shipped-process `arany exec` starts on the named Linux host, each with two distinct 32 KiB included Workspace files and the checked loopback custom Provider. The external runner timestamps spawn and the server's first accepted Provider connection. Engine commits `RunStarted` and `AgentSpawned` before any Provider call, so this is a conservative upper bound for the researched spawn-to-`RunStarted` threshold. The server verifies both full inputs, finishes the Run, and the parent strictly replays the committed Session outside the timer. Report p50/p95/max and require the upper-bound p95 at or below 50 ms; a miss requires direct instrumentation before changing product code.

Security pass: exact local profile conformance precedes disclosure, each process has a cleared environment with a synthetic key and an explicit working directory, and state remains private outside the Workspace. The two files are fixed-size synthetic bytes; the request parser has a 128 KiB cap, the accepted-time channel has one slot, all child processes have parent deadlines and kill/reap cleanup, and the server accepts a fixed number of bounded requests. No remote destination, paid Provider, Tool, or trace export is involved.

Verification map: keep the startup fixture in a focused private performance subfile, compile it in the default untimed suite, run the explicit release measurement, and verify exact output, included-input size, a fresh finished Session, and strict replay for every sample. Then run formatting, strict Clippy, offline tests, package inspection, changed-source review, and auto-learning. Cancellation and Linux RSS are measured separately above; OTLP, native macOS, paid Provider, and advisory gates remain open. No commits during this goal.

Earlier contended-host evidence (2026-10-01): four release activations of the unchanged 100-sample product-process gate failed the 50 ms p95 threshold at 52.612, 54.941, 53.334, and 52.582 ms. Two further unchanged runs failed at 77.392 and 68.416 ms p95; their approximate spawn-to-`RunStarted` creation p95 values were 68.827 and 61.617 ms, so the post-start Provider connection alone could not explain the breach. A temporary no-include variant still failed at 78.474 ms p95, ruling out the two 32 KiB includes as the sole cause on this host; it was removed. A separate `--version` process sample took 3–7 ms, but did not exercise Provider or State admission. The host reported load average 47.29 and CPU `some` pressure 91.83% over ten seconds; neither a specific code path nor contention as the sole cause was established then. An earlier temporary early/late split also showed higher late-sample p95 in both measured runs, without isolating State growth from host scheduling. The independent `show` control passed its 25 ms gate at 10.971 ms p95 for 100 Events. The startup gate remained red pending a quiet-host rerun. No credential or paid Provider was used.

A temporary in-process diagnostic on the same synthetic profile measured 100 iterations each: custom Provider admission p95 was 37.487 and 36.427 ms, StateRoot open/admit each at most 0.034 ms p95, and Engine open at most 3.662 ms p95. Ten stage samples placed 16–32 ms in `CustomTransport::new`, which builds a new Reqwest client; profile parsing and numeric-loopback resolution were negligible. The pinned Reqwest 0.13.5 builder creates a `rustls-platform-verifier` 0.7.1 verifier, whose Linux implementation loads system roots for each verifier. This identifies client construction as a costly observed stage under host load above 50 and CPU `some` pressure above 94%, but does not isolate certificate loading from other client setup or prove a quiet-host regression. Do not disable system trust or special-case the synthetic HTTP benchmark to clear the gate. Both temporary diagnostics were removed; the product and release threshold are unchanged.

Diagnostic method: the ignored startup owner compares its monotonic spawn-to-Provider-accept interval with the committed `RunStarted` and primary `AgentSpawned` Events' wall-clock creation times. The first precedes `RunStarted` append acknowledgement; Engine creates the second only after that acknowledgement, so together they bracket it approximately. The diagnostic is discarded if the runner and listener wall-clock span materially disagrees with their monotonic span; the monotonic gate remains authoritative. This adds no product flag or Event field.

Diagnostic result (2026-10-01): the same ignored owner reports the `RunStarted` Event's creation time before its append acknowledgement and the primary `AgentSpawned` Event's creation time after that acknowledgement, alongside the unchanged monotonic Provider-accept gate. It discards its wall-clock diagnostic unless all 100 samples agree with the monotonic span within 5 ms. Two contended-host runs passed that comparability check but failed the 50 ms gate: the first had `AgentSpawned`/accept p95 of 50.904/53.001 ms; the second had `RunStarted`/`AgentSpawned`/accept p95 of 49.526/52.366/55.231 ms. The latter bracketed the real acknowledgement across 50 ms rather than proving a pass or regression. Host load remained about 34 with CPU `some` pressure about 77%. Transport-after-spawn was only a small part of the observed excess. No paid credential or Provider was used.

Contended current-code repeat (2026-10-01): two further 100-sample release runs of the unchanged synthetic product-process gate failed at Provider-accept p95 53.930 and 55.234 ms. Their `RunStarted` creation p95 was 48.121 and 49.299 ms, and primary `AgentSpawned` creation p95 was 51.567 and 51.603 ms; the post-spawn transport gap remained small. The host had load average 35.90 on 16 visible CPUs and CPU `some` pressure averaging 76.74% over ten seconds. The same gate had previously passed at 16.944–18.301 ms p95 on this host, so those red runs did not establish a product regression. No code, threshold, paid call, or commit changed for this diagnostic.

Host recheck (2026-10-01): load remained about 50 on 16 visible CPUs. A read-only inventory found 45 older `arany` processes with deleted PTYs and deleted executable images; all accumulated CPU time during a ten-second sample, totaling 164 CPU seconds. This was enough active load to invalidate another latency sample on this host, but did not prove which invocation created each process or what a quiet-host Arany p95 would be. No process was touched in that check; the host was rebooted before the later quiet-host measurement.

Quiet-host result (2026-10-01): the unchanged optimized product-process fixture ran two complete 10-warmup/100-measured-start activations after host load dropped below two, with CPU `some` pressure at 0.30% and then 0.00% over ten seconds immediately afterward. Spawn-to-Provider-accept p95 was 14.084 and 14.088 ms, below the fixed 50 ms p95 bound; respective p50/max values were 12.290/15.615 and 11.995/15.068 ms. All 100 Event-clock diagnostics in each activation were comparable; `RunStarted` creation p95 was 12.124 and 12.077 ms. Every sample verified both complete 32 KiB includes, exact product output, one finished Session, and strict replay. This closes the named Linux host gate for the current code and host conditions; it does not prove a cross-platform or continuously unloaded latency guarantee. The earlier contended-host failures remain useful diagnostic evidence, not a current product regression. No credential, paid Provider, code, or commit changed.

### Completed Linux slice: OTLP complete-response deadline

The active opt-in exporter had a [response-deadline finding](../../docs/security/findings/otlp-http-response-deadline.md): Reqwest's blocking adapter restarted its 500 ms timeout for each response read, while OpenTelemetry waited for the entire body. A loopback Collector could hold the sole export worker beyond the declared request bound by trickling bytes. The approved fix keeps the default bounded batch processor and adds one private adapter that drives the async Reqwest request on its own current-thread Tokio runtime, preserving a true whole-response deadline. A direct async-client swap would not work because the SDK worker uses `futures_executor`, and the experimental async SDK processor ignores the supplied shutdown timeout. Neither Engine nor canonical state gains an interface.

Security pass: the untrusted input is the opt-in numeric-loopback Collector's response; only closed typed trace data may leave Arany, with no Provider credential. An absolute 500 ms export deadline must cancel the in-flight async request, drop telemetry on failure, and permit later batches without blocking Run progress. Preserve no proxy, redirect, retry, or automatic decompression, the 256 KiB request cap, 256-span queue, 64-span batch, and 750 ms shutdown bound. This is not a claim about a remote Collector or native macOS.

Verification map: the existing telemetry owner gains one ignored Linux Collector trickle gate that fails under the blocking client and must pass when the second batch is accepted within two seconds while the first response is still trickling. Retain the existing protobuf/privacy, redirect, stalled-Collector, outage, and product-process replay owners; run the focused gate, formatting, strict Clippy, complete offline suite, package verification, selected feature/source review, changed-file review, and auto-learning. Native macOS and release performance remain separate gates. No commit during this goal.

Linux result (2026-10-01): the ignored trickle gate failed on the former blocking transport with `trickling response held the export worker`, reproducing the worker-bound gap without a real credential or Provider call. The first async-adapter attempt failed because its Reqwest future was created outside the runtime; after creating it inside Tokio, the same gate passed in 1.05 seconds. The complete default offline suite also passes. The selected feature graph no longer includes Reqwest `blocking`, and the adapter explicitly disables automatic decompression. Native macOS runtime and release-host measurements remain open. No commit was made.

Workspace text, credentials, Provider bodies, Event payloads, and terminal content remain attacker-controlled sensitive data; only typed IDs, phases, outcomes, bounded model identifiers, counts, and committed sequence numbers cross the telemetry interface. The fixed effective controls are no ambient proxy/header/resource detectors, redirects, or remote TLS/auth claim; a 256-span queue drops on overflow, request and shutdown deadlines are bounded, and every exporter failure is noncanonical and nonfatal after a Run starts. Children inherit no new authority from traces. Invalid explicit configuration fails before `RunStarted` with exit 2; absent configuration starts no worker.

The maximal currently mapped 64-span, 32-events-per-span batch fits the configured 256 KiB limit. ### Implemented slice: stable Linux account root (native gate open)

The fixed keyring slot is per OS user, so a second lock alone would leave different backend markers and plaintext fallbacks under environment-selected roots. On Linux, use the effective UID's passwd home plus `.local/state/arany` as the one account marker/lock root; leave `default_path` and `--state-dir` for Sessions. A validated old API-account or ChatGPT-registration record is copied while holding its old lock and the stable-root lock, then replaced at the old location with an unsupported version-zero tombstone. ### Completed offline slice: reuse a selected ChatGPT account in setup

When an OS-user ChatGPT account is already selected, `/setup` and `--setup` should offer a short, keyboard-selectable choice to use it for model/effort configuration or connect a new account. The saved path reads only the validated local selected UUID at the choice step; no listener, browser, registration, keyring probe, or paid call begins until the user chooses an action. A missing account keeps the existing first-sign-in path. Reusing a saved account invokes the same bounded visible catalog, explicit effort, and separately accepted three-call conformance as a newly saved account, without making a catalog row runnable. Cancelling or declining a reused account's model/check flow leaves previous Session defaults untouched; cancelling after saving a new account retains account-only defaults. A corrupt account index fails closed rather than silently registering another account. No new Provider or UI module is needed.

Security pass: the local account index is selection metadata, not credential authority. The catalog and check reopen the selected consent-matched token and may serialize a near-expiry refresh; a concurrent account change must fail the existing exact-ID comparison before Session defaults are pinned. Existing fixed endpoints, no-proxy/redirect/retry, request/stream limits, and no API-key fallback remain. The selector receives only compiled labels and the opaque account ID, never a token or Workspace bytes. Cancelling before an effect preserves the current Session; an already-started catalog or check has its existing cancellation limits.

Verification map: extend the setup-frame owner at 16/40/50/80 columns in monochrome for both saved-account choices and visible focus; retain existing linear choice parser and catalog/check tests. Add a focused setup decision test where practical without a real account or test-only Provider endpoint, then run formatting, strict Clippy, all offline tests, dependency policy, optimized build, and changed-source review. Update CLI, ChatGPT, terminal, architecture, and setup guidance. Real OAuth, paid conformance, and native macOS remain separate gates. No commit.

Result: the attached setup offers `Use saved` and `Connect new` when the local selected ChatGPT account index is valid. Reuse enters the bounded catalog without reopening OAuth; it commits Session defaults only after the exact model-and-effort check, while cancellation leaves prior defaults intact. A newly connected account still retains account-only defaults on cancellation. The product-process screen-reader PTY test used a private synthetic account index and missing D-Bus socket to prove the reuse choice, fail-closed missing-token handling, unchanged selected index, no new Session, and exact TTY restoration without touching a real account. Monochrome setup frames include this step at 16/40/50/80 columns. Formatting, 268 active offline tests, strict all-target Clippy, offline locked `cargo-deny`, no-fetch `cargo-audit`, and optimized build pass. `cargo-deny` reports only duplicate-version warnings; the temporary Crossterm patch emits one upstream-source parentheses warning. ### Completed offline slice: reauthorize the selected ChatGPT account

Offer `Reconnect` alongside `Use saved` and `Connect new` when a ChatGPT account is selected. The [official returning flow](https://developers.openai.com/siwc/token-sharing-open-source/sign-in) permits reauthorization with the saved issued client ID and no `id_token_hint`: the browser shows its account selector, and Arany must reject a different signed subject. This avoids putting a retained ID token in the browser launcher's process arguments. The selected-account index supplies only the validated UUID, host, issued client, subject, and pinned storage backend at choice time; no old token, keyring read, refresh, registration, or Provider call is needed to offer the action. After the existing backend-specific warning and typed consent, reuse the one-shot callback, fresh state/nonce/PKCE, compiled endpoints, strict redemption, and same-account identity check. Under the account lock, require the original selected UUID/client/backend before replacing the protected record; clear its model evidence and preserve its UUID. Cancellation before save leaves defaults and credentials unchanged; cancellation after verified replacement leaves account-only Session defaults until a new explicit model/effort check. Keep `Connect new` separate and do not add an endpoint, dependency, or generic auth seam.

Security pass: assets are the per-user account index, host/client/subject binding, protected access/refresh/ID tokens, risk receipt, model evidence, and Session defaults. The browser account selector, callback query, token response, and concurrent account replacement are untrusted; only the verified signed ID-token subject plus exact consent and locked selected-registration comparison authorize replacement. The browser process receives a compiled HTTPS URL with no credential hint. The token endpoint and keyring helper retain their fixed destination/transport, bounded body, timeout, and no-output contracts. The one-shot listener remains available only after consent, with existing connection/header/request limits; no Workspace bytes or paid inference call enter reauthorization. A denied or mismatched attempt cannot reach token replacement. An interrupted keyring write may have changed the OS item, so existing-account replacement first publishes a pending marker that blocks token use until verified recovery; never claim rollback. Full live OAuth and native macOS proof remain separate.

Verification map: extend the existing returning-attempt and account-owner tests for a no-hint URL, exact callback client and signed subject, metadata-only target lookup even with an unusable old token, locked selected-ID/backend check before either storage write, same UUID and cleared evidence after successful replacement, and unchanged state after refusal or drift. Extend monochrome 16/40/50/80-column setup frames and one screen-reader PTY choice path without a real account or OAuth redirect. Run formatting, strict Clippy, full offline tests, dependency policy, optimized build, and source review; update CLI, terminal, ChatGPT, setup, and architecture guidance. No real credentials, paid calls, or commits.

Result: `Reconnect` now uses only the selected account's validated metadata until explicit backend-specific consent; its returning URL omits the ID-token hint, uses fresh state/nonce/PKCE, and preserves client/host/signed-subject checks. Under the account lock, verified replacement requires the same selected UUID and client, rejects backend/subject drift, preserves that UUID, and clears model evidence. Existing keyring replacement persists a pending marker before its write so an uncertain result cannot authorize token use; a verified retry clears it. Synthetic account-owner tests cover selection drift, pending keyring metadata lookup, failed replacement, and recovery. The screen-reader product-process PTY covers `Use saved` and `Reconnect` under an unavailable private D-Bus address, confirming no browser, no Session, unchanged selection, and TTY restoration; monochrome frames cover 16/40/50/80 columns. Formatting, 269 active offline tests, strict all-target Clippy, frozen `cargo-deny`, no-fetch `cargo-audit`, package inventory, and optimized Linux build pass. `cargo-deny` has duplicate-version warnings only, and the temporary Crossterm patch has one compiler warning. Live browser OAuth, paid model checks, a direct ChatGPT Engine turn, and native macOS remain unverified. No real credential, paid call, or commit was used.

## Exit criteria

**Current beta 1 implementation goal:** build and launch the local Linux executable, polish account/Provider and joint model/effort selection, and provide a readable chat interface with visible input, understandable errors, predictable pickers, and preserved editing/reading position. Existing automated synthetic scenarios must prove the official ChatGPT consent flow, current account identity/consent admission without mandatory inference, exact custom-destination conformance, durable direct/delegated turns and strict replay, cancellation, resource bounds, credential isolation, and failed-turn behavior. `cargo-deny` and the local build must pass. The user performs real sign-in, plan-consuming calls, remaining native credential checks, and manual UX/accessibility tests after implementation; these pending checks do not block this implementation goal and do not become verified support claims. An unavailable Anthropic subscription route remains an explicit external authorization gap.

**After beta 1:** native macOS, a redistributable Linux/macOS artifact with full notice/provenance review, broader manual accessibility and remaining terminal-fault checks, and non-critical performance work remain separate beta 2/release steps. A local executable is not a distribution approval. Documentation describes only shipped behavior, and durable findings update their narrow authoritative document. No commit during this goal.

- 2026-09-29: Added explicit bounded Engine compaction through the existing Provider seam. `ContextCompacted` records digest-bound success or typed failure with provenance and usage; later Runs pin and consume the newest valid provider-neutral summary. File-backed tests prove reopen, provider switch, failure fallback, fork exclusion, invalid snapshot rejection, and input/output limits. CLI and automatic compaction remain open.
- 2026-09-29: Added the first native OpenAI Responses adapter in a private Provider file, limited to explicit `gpt-5.4` and `OPENAI_API_KEY`. Offline fixtures cover strict structured outcomes, compaction, privacy flags, omitted data, and malformed/refused/incomplete responses. Disabled reqwest's default protocol retries. No CLI command or paid live conformance claim exists yet; the new HTTP/TLS dependency tree still needs release supply-chain review.
- 2026-09-29: Added noninteractive `exec` with explicit native Provider/model, collaboration policy, optional explicit Session append, bounded objective, and durable close/reopen before output. Text gives a sanitized answer and receipt; JSONL emits only the invocation's Events. File-backed scripted tests cover new/append/failure and literal prefixes; product-process tests cover missing key, unsupported model, argument errors, and state/Workspace overlap. Native paid live success, signal cancellation, and cross-process same-Session exclusion remain open.
- 2026-09-29: Split private state locking into `store/state/lock.rs` and held a no-follow per-Session advisory lock through Run, source fork, and manual compaction. The file-backed gated scenario proves busy rejection before Workspace input and Event append, independent-Session progress, cancellation release, and retry; lock-file tests cover unsafe objects and pre-commit cleanup. Native cross-process/macOS behavior and abnormal-death orphan quotas remain release gates.
- 2026-09-29: Added a supervised Linux product-process gate: a separate Engine process holds a scripted Run, shipped `arany exec` fails busy before a deliberately missing Workspace include, and killing the holder releases the lock while canonical replay marks its Run interrupted. macOS behavior and abnormal-death orphan quotas remain release gates.
- 2026-09-29: Added an offline-tested native Anthropic Messages adapter for explicit `claude-sonnet-5` and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, with schema-constrained Run/compaction output, bounded no-retry HTTP, strict completed-text decoding, and CLI credential isolation cases. Paid live direct/team conformance and native platform gates remain open.
- 2026-09-29: Split the private Store implementation into journal and replay files while preserving its single interface and owner thread. Added pure bounded status/activity rows and a Ratatui inline frame with a raw-mode owner; focused width/hostile-text tests pass. Pinned Ratatui 0.29/Crossterm 0.28 for the smaller compiled dependency surface; supply-chain review and PTY restoration remain open. Enabled Tokio time and I/O in the product runtime for Run deadlines and native HTTP adapters.
- 2026-09-29: Added Engine-owned empty Session creation and `SessionDefaultChanged` as an idle-only, full next-Run selection snapshot. File-backed reopen proves the empty Session and defaults; overlap and malformed/active-change cases fail without a valid default Event. The attached command parser, input loop, and native PTY evidence remain open.
- 2026-09-29: Added a bounded named terminal-input reader with typed key/resize actions and guaranteed close/join on terminal drop. Added the closed 15-name slash registry with `/clear`/`/exit` aliases, exact column-zero parsing, `//` escape, completion, lifecycle availability, and local unknown-command suggestions. The parser and key mapping have pure tests; the attached CLI handler and PTY/signal gates remain open.
- 2026-09-29: Split private terminal implementation into `input.rs` and `view.rs`, keeping acquisition, redraw coordination, and restoration in `terminal.rs`. This is a source-layout refactor; public behavior and security boundaries are unchanged.
- 2026-09-29: Added the first bare attached command path: TTY-first admission, durable new Session, composer submission through selected native Provider and cancellable Engine Run, private local controls, and restored-terminal answer/receipt output only after read-only Run confirmation. A positional prompt preflights its selected credential/model before state creation. A Linux PTY smoke proved Session creation, `/provider` and `/model` default changes, clean `/quit`, and missing-key cleanup; the no-TTY product-process test proves no state creation. Remaining Session commands, active inspection, screen-reader, low-height fallback, signals, and cross-platform PTY gates remain open.
- 2026-09-29: Moved draft editing into a private terminal composer with an 8 KiB limit, whole-grapheme navigation/deletion/insertion, and a sanitized horizontal window centered near the cursor. Pure input and TestBackend frame cases cover Unicode, capacity, hostile display text, scrolling, and cursor position; native PTY keyboard coverage remains a release gate.
- 2026-09-29: Added Engine-owned exact-ID Session resume and rename, plus Provider-free fork at a committed Run boundary. Attached startup accepts `--resume ID` and `--fork ID`; idle commands `/resume ID`, `/fork [ID]`, and `/rename TITLE` switch only after durable success. File-backed reopen and wrong-Workspace cases, CLI handler tests, strict lint, offline tests, and a Linux PTY rename/exit/fresh-process-resume smoke cover the slice. Security pass: no Provider credential or Workspace content is needed for local lifecycle commands; Run-pinned Workspace identity is checked before a switch or write. Empty Sessions still lack a persisted Workspace identity, so latest-Workspace `--continue` remains open. Comprehensive PTY and native platform gates remain open.
- 2026-09-29: Added explicit advisory unlock on Session guard drop after repeated parallel offline runs exposed sporadic self-contention; rapid reacquisition and 30 consecutive workspace test passes cover the release path. The exact kernel/file-descriptor timing remains unproven, so native cross-process gates stay open.
- 2026-09-29: Versioned `SessionStarted` payloads now atomically pin Workspace device/inode for new empty and Run-created Sessions while v1 title-only history remains readable. Reduction rejects later Run identity drift; fork resolution rejects inherited/local identity mismatch. File-backed tests cover empty-Session wrong-Workspace denial, v2 reopen, exact `show` JSONL, malformed version/payload, and mismatched Run admission. Security pass: the user-requested Workspace remains the authority; persisted identity only narrows which Session may resume and never selects a Provider or reads Workspace content. Event size stays under the existing 64 KiB cap, and malformed or unsupported history fails closed. Session discovery and `--continue` selection still need a bounded Store query and product-process proof.
- 2026-09-29: Added bounded canonical-Event discovery and attached `--continue`. One Store-thread scan validates global Event encoding, tracks at most 65,536 Session heads, resolves fork Workspace identity, and fully replays the newest matching Session; no mutable index is trusted. The Engine re-admits Workspace identity under exact-ID resume before exposing the Session. File-backed cases cover empty Sessions, multiple Workspaces, latest committed activity, legacy unbound exclusion, forks, no match, and malformed-history failure; attached CLI composition reuses the same selected Session. Security pass: the user-selected Workspace is authority, journal text is untrusted data, no Provider credential or Workspace content is read during discovery, inherited authority cannot widen, and overflow or corruption fails closed with an exact-ID fallback. Native product-process continuation was proved separately above; macOS and performance gates remain open.
- 2026-09-29: Wired idle attached `/compact` to the selected native Provider and Engine's committed digest-bound compaction. The CLI reports typed status and usage without projecting the Provider summary; Ctrl+C reports local interruption and reloads history because a queued append may still commit. File-backed tests cover Workspace rejection before egress and existing success/failure replay; a cleared-environment Linux PTY showed missing-key failure and no compaction Event. Paid live success and in-flight interruption, macOS PTY, and automatic threshold handling remain open.
- 2026-09-29: Reused bounded canonical discovery for Workspace-scoped, recency-ordered Session listing and added keyboard picker entry through `/sessions`, `/resume`, and bare `--resume`. Enter revalidates via exact-ID resume; Escape preserves the current Session. File-backed tests cover order, rename, isolation, empty and malformed states; TestBackend covers inert titles and visible selection; cleared-environment Linux PTY checks switching, cancellation, and fresh-process entry. Adjacent UUIDv7 IDs shared a prefix in the PTY, so picker labels now show their trailing digits. Native macOS, transient mouse, and broader terminal gates remain open.
- 2026-09-29: Added `--screen-reader`/`ARANY_SCREEN_READER=1`, `--no-color`, and pre-raw linear fallback for `TERM=dumb`, short, or unmeasurable terminals. The attached command loop is shared; linear output is labeled, sanitized, append-only, and uses canonical line input plus Unix SIGINT, with a numbered Session picker. Exact pure output fixtures and Linux PTYs cover commands, Session switching, Ctrl+C line separation, environment selection, and six-row fallback. Tokio's signal feature reuses the existing `signal-hook-registry` dependency; no new crate was introduced. Native macOS, manual VoiceOver/Orca, full signal/suspend, and restoration gates remain open.
- 2026-09-29: Replaced Crossterm key polling in linear mode with a dedicated bounded canonical-line reader after PTYs showed multi-kilobyte lines stalling; Crossterm documents raw mode as necessary for reliable key events. Mio was already transitive and is now a direct Unix dependency for stoppable readiness; stdin nonblocking flags are restored after the reader joins to avoid signal-flush shutdown hangs. Linux PTYs prove 2–3 KiB submission, visible 4,095-byte rejection, queued-command order, numbered selection, and Ctrl+C. Prompts now occupy complete lines to reduce queued-input/notice fusion. Platform-specific linear limits are intentionally below the 8 KiB objective contract until a later input-parity slice; macOS native proof remains open.
- 2026-09-30: Added canonical Ctrl+D continuation for linear drafts while Enter remains the submit boundary. The reader validates each physical segment and the aggregate 8 KiB limit before forwarding characters; rejected segments retain the preceding draft and do not automatically submit a prefix. Linux PTY checks show an exact 8 KiB draft accepted, a 1-byte overflow rejected, a shorter retry appended to the retained draft, and Ctrl+C clearing it. Pure boundary tests cover submission versus continuation, physical and aggregate limits, controls, and invalid UTF-8. Native macOS and manual screen-reader review remain release gates.
- 2026-09-30: Routed Unix SIGTSTP and inline Ctrl+Z through one terminal-owned release, self-SIGSTOP, and reacquisition sequence. Linux PTYs showed the process stopped with canonical echo restored, then resumed with raw mode and a retained inline draft; a linear 6,000-byte draft still rejected a 2,193-byte segment after resume and accepted 2,192 bytes. The line reader now restores its byte count from the composer on reacquisition, preventing a post-resume limit bypass. The PTY `script` host must be resumed as a job before Arany can reacquire; resuming only its child produces a false terminal error. SIGTERM, active-Run suspend, restoration faults, and macOS remain release gates.
- 2026-09-30: Added Unix SIGTERM/SIGHUP routing through attached idle, picker, compaction, and Run input, plus a three-second post-cancellation shutdown bound for signals and second Ctrl+C. A receipt is emitted only after terminal restoration and read-only confirmation of a terminal Run. Direct Linux `script` PTYs showed SIGTERM in inline mode restored the exact `stty -g` settings, SIGHUP exited linear mode, and SIGHUP exited a linear Session picker. Active-Run signal/second-press process evidence, fault injection, macOS, and signals during the brief restored-output/reacquisition interval remain open.
- 2026-09-30: Kept the terminal owner and its original Tokio signal listeners alive while confirmed Run output is emitted, then transferred those listeners to the reacquired owner; suspend/resume now preserves them too. This closes the listener-drop interval in code, though a controlled signal-during-output PTY case remains open. Moved Session picker input into private `src/cli/attached/picker.rs` without adding a new public seam; existing tests and a linear PTY `/sessions` → close → `/quit` journey passed. Active-Run signal proof, macOS, and broader release gates remain open.
- 2026-09-30: Added optional one-slot `RunProgress` observation from acknowledged Events. The Session reducer now shares its validated incremental transition with live projection while closed replay retains interrupted-prefix semantics. Attached inline and linear modes redraw on committed snapshots without Provider-stream content or duplicate Store replay. Existing gated team and new-Session cancellation scenarios verify active and terminal snapshots against durable Events; the exact linear fixture covers active labels and inert output. Native active-Run PTY, active commands/drafts, macOS, and release gates remain open.
- 2026-09-30: Kept in-flight attached drafts in the bounded terminal composer and intercepted active slash commands through a private read-only/locked handler. Enter on an objective retains it for a later explicit idle submission; the linear reader restores the retained byte count after Enter and output reacquisition. A command corpus and canonical-line boundary extension cover non-egress and overflow behavior. Native active-Run PTY, full agent details, macOS, and release gates remain open.
- 2026-09-30: Added a read-only, versioned custom `openai-responses` profile loader over `provider-profiles.json` in the admitted state root. It bounds file and profile counts, rejects unsafe file objects and malformed or noncanonical destinations, and binds each credential reference to one origin. No credential is read and no custom Run is admitted; data-free conformance, expiring evidence, address pinning, and product-process proof remain open.

A retired command rejects locally with a singular suggestion; active `/model` remains view-only. Update the [terminal contract](../../docs/specs/terminal.md), setup guidance and next steps. No new adapter, module, dependency, state format, credential operation or inference is needed.

Verification map: extend the existing frame corpus for role/rule styling at narrow and normal widths, body sanitization and monochrome readability; retain history anchor/count owners. Update existing registry/composer/help and active-submission corpora, plus linear/inline catalog and slash-completion product journeys, to prove singular selection, retired-command rejection, retained drafts and terminal restoration. Reuse committed-answer/linear owners for output attribution and unchanged headless bytes. Run formatting, strict all-target lint, locked offline debug/release tests/builds and source review after edits settle.

Security pass: user/model bodies remain attacker-controlled inert data; trusted role headings and width-bounded rules add no authority. Existing sanitizer, Event/output ceilings, committed-state confirmation and terminal lifecycle retain their ownership. No credential, prompt, network, process or persistence authority changes. The user's real answer closes only the reported selected-route failure; broader compatibility/keyring/platform claims remain separately unverified.
